Beilinson-Drinfeld Schubert varieties of parahoric group schemes and twisted global Demazure modules
Jiuzu Hong, Huanhuan Yu

Abstract
. Let 𝒢 \mathcal{G} be a parahoric Bruhat-Tits group scheme arising from a Γ \Gamma -curve C C and a certain Γ \Gamma -action on a simple algebraic group G G for some finite cyclic group Γ \Gamma . We prove the flatness of Beilinson-Drinfeld Schubert varieties of 𝒢 \mathcal{G} , we determine the rigidified Picard group of the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian Gr 𝒢 , C n {\rm Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}} of 𝒢 \mathcal{G} , and we establish the factorizable and equivariant structures on rigidified line bundles over Gr 𝒢 , C n {\rm Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}} . We develop an algebraic theory of global Demazure modules of twisted current algebras, and using our geometric results we prove that when C = 𝔸 1 C=\mathbb{A}^{1} , the spaces of global sections of line bundles on BD Schubert varieties of 𝒢 \mathcal{G} are dual to the twisted global Demazure modules. This generalizes the work of Dumanski-Feigin-Finkelberg in the untwisted setting.



1.
Introduction

Let
G
be a simple algebraic group of adjoint type over
\mathbb{C}
, and let
\mathfrak{g}
be its Lie algebra. We fix a Borel subgroup
B
and a maximal torus
T\subseteq B
. Let
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}
be the affine Grassmannian of
G
. A (spherical) affine Schubert variety in
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}
is parametrized by a dominant coweight
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)^{+}
of
G
, denoted by
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda}
. Let
\mathscr{L}
be the level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}
, and let
\mathscr{L}^{c}
be its
c
-th tensor power for some
c>0
. Then, the space
H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda},\mathscr{L}^{c})
of global sections is dual to a level
c
affine Demazure module
D(c,\lambda)
, cf.
[
26
]
. By the work of Fourier-Littelmann
[
20
]
, when
G
is of type
A,D,E
, there is a fusion product
*
on Demazure modules with the property that
D(c,\lambda)*D(c,\mu)\simeq D(c,\lambda+\mu)
for any two dominant coweights
\lambda,\mu
of
G
. When
c=1
,
D(1,\lambda)
is a Weyl module. The Weyl modules and Demazure modules have global counterparts, defined in
[
18
]
and
[
19
,
9
]
respectively. Similarly, the global counterparts of affine Grassmanians and affine Schubert varieties are Beilinson-Drinfeld (BD) Grassmannians and BD Schubert varieties, which play a central role in geometric Langlands program. In
[
10
]
, Dumanski-Feigin-Finkelberg proved that there is a duality between the spaces of global sections of level
c
line bundle on BD Schubert varieties and the level
c
global Demazure modules of the current algebra
\mathfrak{g}[t]
, where under this duality the factorization of line bundles is compatible with the factorization of global Demazure module. The fusion product of Demazure modules has actually been encoded in the factorization of line bundles on BD Schubert varieties, in the sense that the fusion of Demazure modules appears in a fiber of global Demazure module.

Let
\sigma
be a standard automorphism on
G
of order
m
preserving
B
and
T
, where
\sigma
is simply a diagram automorphism if
G
is not of type
A_{2n}
, see Section
2.2
. We consider the twisted current algebra
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
, where
\sigma
acts on
\mathfrak{g}
and
\sigma
acts on
t
by
\sigma(t)=e^{-\frac{2\pi\rm{i}}{m}}t
. In fact,
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
is isomorphic to a special current algebra or hyperspecial current algebra defined in
[
8
,
27
]
, see
[
27
, Section 4.2]
and Theorem
2.2
.
The fusion theories of twisted Demazure modules and twisted Weyl modules are extensively studied, e.g. in
[
16
,
15
,
8
,
27
]
. Let
X_{*}(T)^{+}_{\sigma}
be the set of
\sigma
-coinvariants of all dominant coweights of
G
. For each
\bar{\lambda}\in X_{*}(T)^{+}_{\sigma}
, we denote by
D^{\sigma}(c,\bar{\lambda})
the level
c
twisted Demazure module of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
. Similar to the untwisted case,
D^{\sigma}(c,\bar{\lambda})
is dual to the global section of a level
c
line bundle on the twisted Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\mathscr{G}}
of the special parahoric group scheme
\mathscr{G}={\rm Res}_{\mathcal{O}/\bar{\mathcal{O}}}(G_{\mathcal{O}})^{\sigma}
, see Section
4.4
. In
[
4
]
, Besson-Hong determined the smooth locus of
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\bar{\lambda}}_{\mathscr{G}}
, where the Demazure modules of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
and the global Schubert variety of a parahoric Bruhat-Tits group scheme
\mathcal{G}:={\rm Res}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}(G_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})^{\sigma}
are crucially used.

Our original purpose of this paper is to develop an algebraic theory of twisted global Demazure modules of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
, and generalize the work
[
10
]
to the twisted setting, i.e. to establish a duality between twisted global Demazure modules of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
and the spaces of global sections of line bundles on BD Schubert varieties of
\mathcal{G}
. In order to establish this duality, the major part of our work is devoted to proving the flatness of BD Schubert varieties of
\mathcal{G}
, and establishing factorizable and equivariant structures on the rigidified line bundles over the BD Grassmannians, for more general parahoric Bruhat-Tits group scheme
\mathcal{G}
. On this part, we closely follow the works
[
37
,
38
,
39
]
of Zhu. In fact, our work also provides proofs of some geometric ingredients needed in
[
10
]
, which were missing in literature.
In spite of the parallels with the untwisted case, there are some essential differences between twisted and untwisted cases. For example, the twisted theory over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is already very involved. We need to employ many new ideas and techniques to take care of ramifications.

Let
\Gamma
be the finite group acting on
G
generated by
\sigma
, and let
C
be an irreducible smooth algebraic curve over
\mathbb{C}
with a faithful
\Gamma
-action such that all ramified points in
C
are totally ramified. Let
\bar{C}
be the quotient curve
C/\Gamma
. Let
\mathcal{G}
be the
\Gamma
-fixed point subgroup scheme of the Weil restriction group
\mathrm{Res}_{C/\bar{C}}(G\times C)
. Then
\mathcal{G}
is a parahoric Bruhat-Tits group scheme over
\bar{C}
. In Section
4
, we define Beilinson-Drinfeld Schubert varieties of
\mathcal{G}
and prove their flatness.

Let
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
(resp.
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
) denote the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian (resp. the jet group scheme) of
\mathcal{G}
over
C^{n}
. For any
n
-tuple of coweights
\vec{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})
of
G
, we first construct a section
s_{\vec{\lambda}}:C^{n}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
, and then we define the Beilinson-Drinfeld Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
to be the reduced closure of the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-orbit at
s_{\vec{\lambda}}
. The construction of
s_{\vec{\lambda}}
is achieved via a construction of a locally trivial
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor attached to
\vec{\lambda}
. By the Tannakian interpretation of
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor (cf. Theorem
A.13
), the construction of this
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor is reduced to the construction of a
\Gamma
-equivariant tensor functor, see Section
4.2
. The main result of Section
4
is Theorem
4.20
, which asserts that

Theorem 1.1
.

The BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is flat over
C^{n}
. Moreover, the schematic fiber
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
of
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
at each
\vec{p}\in C^{n}
is reduced. More precisely,
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is a product of affine Schubert varieties (possibly twisted).

In the untwisted case, this theorem is due to Zhu, see
[
37
]
for
n=2
. In the twisted case, the main idea of the proof of Theorem
1.1
is similar. When
n=1
, it is also due to Zhu
[
38
]
. For
n>1
, we need a new ingredient suggested by Zhu (by private communication), a generalization of cohomology and base change, which is formulated in Appendix
B
. This new method is of course applicable to the untwisted case. In order to employ Theorem
B.3
, in Section
4.3
we define the convolution variety
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
which admits a proper morphism to
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
. In fact, we give two definitions, one is defined via a sequence of fibrations of global Schubert varieties, and the other one is defined as the reduced closure of
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-orbit at a section
\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}}
of the convolution Grassmannian. In Proposition
4.16
, we show that these two definitions are the same. The first definition tells that
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is flat over
C^{n}
, and the second definition tells that the image of
\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
is exactly
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
. As an application of Theorem
1.1
, we also prove that any BD Schubert variety is normal, see Theorem
4.23
. When
n=1
, this is due to Zhu, cf.
[
38
, Corollary 6.14]
.

Section
5
is devoted to the study of rigidified line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
. Let
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})
denote the Picard group of rigidified line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
, i.e. the line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
together with a trivialization along a distinguished section
e
over
C^{n}
, see Section
5.1
. We prove the following result in Theorem
5.8
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Suppose that
G
is simply-connected.

(1)

For any
n\geq 1
, the central charge map
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})\to\mathbb{Z}
is an isomorphism.

(2)

Any line bundle in
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})
has factorizable property.

The proof of the injectivity of the central charge map is similar to the proof in the untwisted case, cf.
[
39
, Section 3]
. In the untwisted case, the level one line bundle can be obtained by pulling-back the level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
over a projective smooth curve. However, in the twisted case the existence of the level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}
is not known yet (actually it may not exist in certain cases). Nevertheless, there is a level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
constructed in
[
4
]
, and we are able to use this line bundle to construct line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
.
Our idea of the proof of Theorem
1.2
is as follows.
For a general
\Gamma
-curve
C
, given any ramified point
p\in C
, there is a
\Gamma
-stable neighborhood
U
of
p
which admits a
\Gamma
-equivariant map to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Then, the pull-back of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
gives a level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},U^{n}\setminus Z}
for some
Z
closed in
U^{n}
, cf. Lemma
5.6
. These line bundles together with the level one line bundle over the unramified part (cf. Proposition
5.7
) can be glued via rigidity and extended to a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
, which generates
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})
. Moreover, this line bundle satisfies the factorization property. When
n>1
, we crucially use a relative version of seesaw principle (cf. Lemma
5.1
).

Another main result of Section
5
is Theorem
5.15
, which asserts that

Theorem 1.3
.

With the same assumption as in Theorem
1.2
, there is a unique
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant stucture on the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
.

The hard part of this theorem is the existence. In the proof, we first construct a
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\rm det}
by embedding
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
to a BD Grassmannian of
GL(\mathcal{V})
for some vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
over
C
. By the étale base change property of BD Grassmannians (cf. Corollary
4.8
), we are reduced to the case
C=\mathbb{A}^{1}
. In this case, the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\rm det}
has a rigidified structure, and hence is a power of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, where Theorem
1.2
is crucially used. This in fact implies that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
admits a
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure. This step is guaranteed by Proposition
5.10
, whose proof is quite technical. We first prove this proposition when
n=1
and
n=2
separately, and for general case we use Hartogs’ Lemma. In our argument, we need to restrict line bundles to BD Schubert varieties so that all arguments are in the finite type geometry.

To prove Theorem
1.5
(or Theorem
6.5
) in full generality, we need to work with adjoint type group
G
. In this case, it is difficult (or impossible) to construct a factorizable level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
for a general
\Gamma
-curve
C
. Nevertheless, when
C=\mathbb{A}^{1}
with the standard action of
\Gamma
. We are able to construct level one line bundles on BD Schubert varieties which admits factorization properties compatible with the factorization of BD Schubert varieties, see Proposition
5.18
. Moreover, using Theorem
1.3
we are able to prove that these line bundles admit
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structures, where
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{C^{n}}
is the jet group scheme arising from the simply-connected cover of
G
, see Proposition
5.19
.

As mentioned earlier, the main motivation of this work is to establish a relation between twisted global Demazure modules and line bundles on BD Schubert varieties of
\mathcal{G}
.
In Section
3.2
, for any
\vec{\lambda}\in(X_{*}(T)^{+})^{n}
, we define the twisted global Demazure module
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})
of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
. Using the results of Fourier-Littelmann
[
20
]
and Kus-Vankatesh
[
27
]
, we prove the following result in Theorem
3.12
and Theorem
3.13
.

Theorem 1.4
.

Given
\vec{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{n})\in(X_{*}(T)^{+})^{n}
, let
\bar{\lambda}
be the image of
\sum\lambda_{i}
in
X_{*}(T)^{+}_{\sigma}
.

(1)

The fiber
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{0}
at
0\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
is the twisted Demazure module
D^{\sigma}(c,\bar{\lambda})
.

(2)

The twisted global Demazure module
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})
is a graded free
\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})
-module, where
\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})
is the weight algebra associated to the cyclic generator of
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})
.

As a consequence of Theorem
1.1
and Proposition
5.19
, the space
H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
of global sections of the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
is a
(\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma},\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}])
-bimodule. In Section
6.1
, we further equip a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, which follows that
H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
is a graded free
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-module, see Theorem
6.4
. Finally, we prove the following Borel-Weil type theorem in Theorem
6.5
.

Theorem 1.5
.

There exists an isomorphism of
(\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma},\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}])
-bimodules

\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\simeq H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{c}\big)^{\vee},

where
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}=\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
. Moreover,
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
has factorization property and it is compatible with the factorization of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
under the above isomorphism.

Our proof is similar to the proof in
[
10
]
, while there are some distinctions, see Remark
6.6
. As an application of this theorem, the fiber
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\vec{p}}
at any
\vec{p}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
can be determined, see Corollary
6.7
and Remark
6.8
. In fact, the fiber
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\vec{p}}
is a tensor product of affine Demazure modules (possibly twisted) depending on the type of the point
\vec{p}
.

The Borel-Weil type theorem for twisted global Weyl module is proved by Braverman-Finkelberg in
[
3
, Theorem 4.6]
, where they work with twisted Zastava spaces. In fact, when the level
c=1
,
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})
is isomorphic to a twisted global Weyl module, see Theorem
3.22
.

Finally, we make a remark when
G
is
A_{2\ell}
. In this case, our automorphism
\sigma
is chosen to be the standard automorphism of order 4. The twisted current algebra
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
corresponds to a special parahoric subalgebra of
A^{(2)}_{2\ell}
. In fact, there is another special parahoric subalgebra of
A^{(2)}_{2\ell}
, which can be realized as the twisted current algebra
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\tau}
corresponding to the diagram automorphism
\tau
of order
2
. For this automorophism
\tau
, all results in Section
4
hold, and in Section
5
similar results hold except that
{\rm Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})
is generated by a level
2
rigidified line bundle. Moreover, we expect that Theorem
1.5
still hold for even levels.
