3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T_{\mathrm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.79448pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\phi\circ\alpha,u\circ\alpha)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 125.8163pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-42.46832pt\raise-15.83778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(q_{\rm red},u\circ\alpha)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.65775pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 125.8163pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern-35.92151pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U:=T^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}\times_{S^{\prime}}S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.89542pt\raise-24.90277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0228pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\phi^{\prime}\circ\alpha^{\prime},u^{\prime}\circ\alpha^{\prime})\times{\mathrm{Id}_{S}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 107.92151pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 107.92151pt\raise-31.67557pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(X\times S^{\prime})\times_{S^{\prime}}S}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Since
(\phi^{\prime}\circ\alpha^{\prime},u^{\prime}\circ\alpha^{\prime})\in(\Delta_{x^{\prime}_{1}}\cup\Delta_{x^{\prime}_{2}})(T^{\prime}_{\rm red})
and
(\Delta_{x^{\prime}_{1}}\cup\Delta_{x^{\prime}_{2}})\times_{S^{\prime}}S\simeq\Delta_{x_{1}}\cup\Delta_{x_{2}}
, we have
(\phi^{\prime}\circ\alpha^{\prime},u^{\prime}\circ\alpha^{\prime})\times{\mathrm{Id}_{S}}\in((\Delta_{x^{\prime}_{1}}\cup\Delta_{x^{\prime}_{2}})\times_{S^{\prime}}S)(U)\simeq(\Delta_{x_{1}}\cup\Delta_{x_{2}})(U)
. Composing with
(q_{\rm red},u\circ\alpha)
, by the commutative diagram (
4.10
) we get
(\phi\circ\alpha,u\circ\alpha)\in(\Delta_{x_{1}}\cup\Delta_{x_{2}})(T_{\rm red})
. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

From now on, we will focus on the parahoric Bruhat-Tits group scheme
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}}=\mathrm{Res}_{C/\bar{C}}\big(G\times C\big)^{\Gamma}
over
\bar{C}
.

Definition 4.7
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}},C^{n}}:=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}},\bar{C}^{n}}\times_{\bar{C}^{n}}C^{n}
the base change of BD grassmannian of
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}}
to
C^{n}
. More precisely, for every
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
R
, we define

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}},C^{n}}(R):=\left\{(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n},\mathcal{F},\beta)\,\middle|\,p_{i}\in C(R),\mathcal{F}\text{ a }\mathcal{G}\text{-torsor on }\bar{C}_{R},\beta:\mathcal{F}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\cup\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\simeq\mathring{\mathcal{F}}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\cup\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\right\}

If there is no confusion, we will simply use the notation
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
.

The BD Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
is an ind-scheme, ind-of-finite type and ind-projective over
C^{n}
, see
[
39
, Remark 3.1.4]
. The following result is a consequence of Proposition
4.4
.

Corollary 4.8
.

(1)

Let
X,Y
be irreducible smooth curves with faithful
\Gamma
-actions. Suppose
f:X\to Y
is an
\Gamma
-equivariant étale morphism such that
\Gamma_{x}=\Gamma_{f(x)}
for any
x\in X
. Let
Z=\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in X^{n}\,|\,\bar{x}_{i}\neq\bar{x}_{j},\overline{f(x_{i})}=\overline{f(x_{j})}\text{ for some }i\neq j\}
. We have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}},Y^{n}}\times_{Y^{n}}(X^{n}\setminus Z).

(2)

Let
C
be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action such that all points are unramified. Let
Z=\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in C^{n}\,|\,x_{i}\neq x_{j},\bar{x}_{i}=\bar{x}_{j}\text{ for some }i\neq j\}
. Then,

\mathrm{Gr}_{G_{C},C^{n}\setminus Z}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}},\bar{C}^{n}}\times_{\bar{C}^{n}}(C^{n}\setminus Z)=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}},C^{n}\setminus Z},

where
G_{C}
is the constant group scheme over
C
.

Proof.

For part (1), by Lemma
4.1
and Proposition
4.2
,
f
induces an étale morphism
\bar{f}:\bar{X}\to\bar{Y}
and there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}}\simeq\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}}\times_{\bar{Y}}\bar{X}
. Let
\bar{Z}=\{(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\in\bar{X}^{n}\,|\,p_{i}\neq p_{j},\bar{f}(p_{i})=\bar{f}(p_{j})\text{ for some }i\neq j\}
. Then
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in Z
if and only if
(\bar{x}_{1},\ldots,\bar{x}_{n})\in\bar{Z}
. By Proposition
4.4
, we have an isomorphism

(4.11)

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},\bar{X}^{n}\setminus\bar{Z}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}},\bar{Y}^{n}}\times_{\bar{Y}^{n}}(\bar{X}^{n}\setminus\bar{Z}).

Note that there are isomorphisms

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z}=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},\bar{X}^{n}}\times_{\bar{X}^{n}}(X^{n}\setminus Z)\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},\bar{X}^{n}}\times_{\bar{X}^{n}}(\bar{X}^{n}\setminus\bar{Z})\times_{\bar{X}^{n}\setminus\bar{Z}}(X^{n}\setminus Z),

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}},Y^{n}}\times_{Y^{n}}(X^{n}\setminus Z)=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}},\bar{Y}^{n}}\times_{\bar{Y}^{n}}(X^{n}\setminus Z)\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}},\bar{Y}^{n}}\times_{\bar{Y}^{n}}(\bar{X}^{n}\setminus\bar{Z})\times_{\bar{X}^{n}\setminus\bar{Z}}(X^{n}\setminus Z).

By base change via
X^{n}\setminus Z\to\bar{X}^{n}\setminus\bar{Z}
, the isomophism (
4.11
) becomes

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{Y}},Y^{n}}\times_{Y^{n}}(X^{n}\setminus Z).

For part (2), we consider the étale morphism
\pi:C\to\bar{C}
. There is a natural isomorphism
G_{C}\simeq\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}}\times_{\bar{C}}C
. By Proposition
4.4
, we get an isomorphism

\mathrm{Gr}_{G_{C},C^{n}\setminus Z}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{C}},\bar{C}^{n}}\times_{\bar{C}^{n}}(C^{n}\setminus Z).\qed

Let
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
be a partition of
[n]
. Similar to Definition
3.15
, we define
C_{\xi}^{n}
to be the following open subset in
C^{n}
,

C_{\xi}^{n}:=\{(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\in C^{n}\mid\bar{p}_{i}\neq\bar{p}_{j},\ \forall i\in I_{\alpha},j\in J_{\beta},\alpha\neq\beta\}.

By
[
39
, Theorem 3.2.1]
,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
and
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
have a factorization property over
C_{\xi}^{n}
as follows

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\times_{C^{n}}C^{n}_{\xi}\simeq\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{j}}}\bigg)\times_{C^{n}}C^{n}_{\xi},

(4.13)

\displaystyle L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle:=L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\times_{C^{n}}C^{n}_{\xi}\simeq\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{k}L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{I_{j}}}\bigg)\times_{C^{n}}C^{n}_{\xi}.

4.2.
Beilinson-Drinfeld Schubert varieties

With the same setup as in Section
4.1
, we define a
\Gamma
-action on
C^{n+1}=C^{n}\times C
by letting
\Gamma
act on the last copy of
C
. More precisely, this action is given by

(4.14)

\gamma(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n},p_{n+1}):=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n},\gamma p_{n+1}),

for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
,
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n},p_{n+1})\in C^{n+1}
.

Let
T
be a
\Gamma
-stable maximal torus in
G
. Given any
\vec{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{n})
with
\lambda_{i}\in X_{*}(T)
, we shall construct a
\Gamma
-equivariant tensor functor
F
from the category
\mathrm{Rep}(T)
of finite dimensional representations of
T
to the category
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(C^{n+1})
of locally free sheaves of finite rank over
C^{n+1}
. For each character
\nu
of
T
, we define

(4.15)

F(\mathbb{C}_{\nu}):=\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\bigg),

where
\Delta_{\gamma,i}:=\{(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n},a_{n+1})\mid a_{n+1}=\gamma a_{i}\}

is a divisor in
C^{n+1}
.

Lemma 4.9
.

This assignment (
4.15
) extends to a tensor functor
F:\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(C^{n+1})
which is
\Gamma
-equivariant in the sense of Definition
A.4
. As a consequence, by Theorem
A.13
, this functor
F
defines a
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor
\mathcal{F}
over
C^{n+1}
.

Proof.

For each
\eta\in\Gamma
, denote by
\eta_{T}:T\to T
the map sending
h
to
\eta(h)
. Let
\eta_{T}^{*}:\mathrm{Rep}(T)\to\mathrm{Rep}(T)
be the pullback functor induced from
\eta_{T}
. Define the
\Gamma
-action on
X^{*}(T)
by

\eta(\nu)(t):=\nu(\eta^{-1}(t)),

where
\eta\in\Gamma
,
\nu\in X^{*}(T)
, and
t\in T
. Then, we have
\eta_{T}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})=\mathbb{C}_{\eta^{-1}(\nu)}
, and

(4.16)

F\eta_{T}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})=\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\eta^{-1}(\nu)\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\bigg)=\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\eta\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\bigg),

for any
\eta\in\Gamma
and
\nu\in X^{*}(T)
.

Let
Y:=C^{n+1}
be the variety with the
\Gamma
-action given in (
4.14
). For each
\eta\in\Gamma
, denote by
\eta_{Y}:Y\to Y
the map sending
y
to
\eta(y)
. Let
\eta_{Y}^{*}:\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
be the pullback functor induced from
\eta_{Y}
. Then, there is an isomorphism

(4.17)

\eta_{Y}^{*}F(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\eta^{-1}\gamma,i}\bigg)=\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\eta\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\bigg).

Combining (
4.16
) and (
4.17
), we have an isomorphism between tensor functors
\theta_{\eta}:F\eta_{T}^{*}\to\eta_{Y}^{*}F
for each
\eta\in\Gamma
. One can check that
F
together with the collection of isomorphisms
\{\theta_{\eta}\}_{\eta\in\Gamma}
satisfy the axioms of Definition
A.4
. This extends to a
\Gamma
-equivariant functor from
\mathrm{Rep}(T)
to
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(C^{n+1})
.
∎

For any
\mathbb{C}
-scheme
X
with a
\Gamma
-action, we denote by
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{X}:=X\times G
the trivial
G
-torsor over
X
with a
\Gamma
-action acting diagonally on
X\times G
, and call it the trivial
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor over
X
. For the general definition of
(\Gamma,G)
-torsors, see Definition
A.1
.

Definition 4.10
.

A
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor
\mathcal{P}
over a scheme
X
is called locally trivial if for any
x\in X
, there exists an open neighborhood
U_{x}
of
x
such that

(1)

U_{x}
is stable under the stabilizer
\Gamma_{x}
of
x
,

(2)

\mathcal{P}|_{U_{x}}
is isomorphic to the trivial
(\Gamma_{x},G)
-torsor
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{U_{x}}
over
U_{x}
.

Note that not every
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor is locally trivial, and in fact this is determined by local types of
(\Gamma,G)
-torsors at ramified points, see
[
11
]
.

Lemma 4.11
.

The
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor
\mathcal{F}
constructed in Lemma
4.9
is locally trivial over
C^{n+1}
.

Proof.

Let
x
be any point in
C^{n+1}
. We will find a
\Gamma
-stable neighborhood
U_{x}
of
x
in
C^{n}
such that
\mathcal{F}
is trivializable over
U_{x}
as
(\Gamma,T)
-torsors.

Let
\Gamma_{x}
be the stabilizer of
x
in
\Gamma
. Given
1\leq i\leq n
, suppose that
x\in\Delta_{\eta_{i},i}
for some
\eta_{i}\in\Gamma
. In this case, we observe that
x\in\Delta_{\gamma,i}
if and only if
\gamma\in\eta_{i}\Gamma_{x}
. Hence there exists an open neighborhood
U_{i}
of
x
with a regular function
f_{i}
over
U_{i}
such that
f_{i}
defines
\Delta_{\eta_{i},i}\cap U_{i}
. Moreover, we can shrink
U_{i}
so that
U_{i}\cap\Delta_{\gamma,i}=\emptyset
for any
\gamma\not\in\eta_{i}\Gamma_{x}
. For the given
i
, if
x\not\in\Delta_{\eta,i}
for any
\eta\in\Gamma
, by convention we take
\eta_{i}=e
,
U_{i}=C^{n+1}\setminus\bigcup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\Delta_{\gamma,i}
, and
f_{i}=1
. Combining the above constructions, for any
x\in C^{n+1}
, we have the following open neighborhood
U_{x}
of
x
in
C^{n+1}
,

U_{x}:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{x}}\gamma U_{i},

and a collection of functions
\{f_{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,n}
defined over
U_{x}
.
Clearly
U_{x}
is
\Gamma_{x}
-stable. Moreover, for any
1\leq i\leq n
and
\gamma\in\eta_{i}\Gamma_{x}
, the regular function
\gamma\eta_{i}^{-1}f_{i}
defines the divisor
\Delta_{\gamma,i}=\gamma\eta_{i}^{-1}\Delta_{\eta_{i},i}
over
U_{x}
.

Now, for each character
\nu
of
T
, denote by
D_{\nu}
the divisor
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}
. Set

f_{\nu}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{\gamma\in\eta_{i}\Gamma_{x}}(\gamma\eta_{i}^{-1}f_{i})^{\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle}.

Then
f_{\nu}
defines the divisor
D_{\nu}
locally in
U_{x}
, since
U_{x}\cap\Delta_{\gamma,i}=\emptyset
for any
\gamma\not\in\eta_{i}\Gamma_{x}
. For any characters
\nu,\omega\in X^{*}(T)
, we have
f_{\nu}\cdot f_{\omega}=f_{\nu+\omega}
. Consider the tensor functor
F_{x}^{0}:\mathrm{Rep}(T)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(U_{x})
sending
\mathbb{C}_{\nu}
to
\mathscr{O}_{U_{x}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}_{\nu}
. It is easy to see
F_{x}^{0}
is
\Gamma_{x}
-equivariant and this gives a trivial
(\Gamma_{x},T)
-torsor
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{U_{x}}
over
U_{x}
. Moreover, there is an isomorphism

\phi_{\nu}:F|_{U_{x}}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})=\mathscr{O}_{U_{x}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\bigg)\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\mathscr{O}_{U_{x}}\simeq F_{x}^{0}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu}),

where the first isomorphism is given by multiplying by
f_{\nu}
. One can check that these isomorphisms
\{\phi_{\nu}\}_{\nu\in X^{*}(T)}
respect the tensor structure since
\mathscr{O}(D_{\nu})\otimes\mathscr{O}(D_{\omega})\simeq\mathscr{O}(D_{\nu+\omega})
. Thus, there is an isomorphism
\phi:F|_{U_{x}}\to F_{x}^{0}
between tensor functors. By Theorem
A.13
,
\phi
induces an isomorphism
\tilde{\phi}:\mathcal{F}|_{U_{x}}\to\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{U_{x}}
between
(\Gamma_{x},T)
-torsors over
U_{x}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{F}
is locally trivial following Definition
4.10
.
∎

Set
U=C^{n+1}\setminus\bigcup_{\gamma,i}\Delta_{\gamma,i}
. Let
F^{0}:\mathrm{Rep}(T)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(U)
be the tensor functor sending
\mathbb{C}_{\nu}
to
\mathscr{O}_{U}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}_{\nu}
. Then,
F^{0}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant, and it gives a trivial
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor
\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{U}
over
U
. For each
\nu\in X_{*}(T)
, there is a natural isomorphism

(4.18)

\eta_{\nu}:F|_{U}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}F^{0}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu}).

Then,
\{\eta_{\nu}\}_{\nu\in T^{*}}
defines an isomorphism
\eta:F|_{U}\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}F^{0}
. By Theorem
A.13
, this is equivalent to an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}|_{U}\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\mathring{\mathcal{F}}_{U}
, which will still be denoted by
\eta
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{G}:=\mathcal{F}\times^{T}G
be the
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor over
C^{n+1}
induced from
\mathcal{F}
. Then,
\eta
induces a trivialization
\eta_{G}
of
\mathcal{F}_{G}
outside
\bigcup_{\gamma,i}\Delta_{\gamma,i}
. By Lemma
4.11
,
\mathcal{F}_{G}
is a locally trivial
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor. We define a morphism
\pi:C^{n+1}\to C^{n}\times\bar{C}
by
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n},p_{n+1})\mapsto(p_{1},\ldots p_{n},\bar{p}_{n+1})
. From the local triviality of
\mathcal{F}_{G}
,
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{G})^{\Gamma}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor over
C^{n}\times\bar{C}
, denoted by
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
. Moreover,
\eta_{G}
induces a trivialization
\bar{\eta}
of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}
outside
\pi\big(\bigcup_{\gamma,i}\Delta_{\gamma,i}\big)=\bigcup_{i}\Delta_{\bar{P}_{i}}
,
where
P_{i}:C^{n}\to C
is the projection map sending
\vec{p}
to
p_{i}
.

We construct a section
s_{\vec{\lambda}}:C^{n}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
as follows:

(4.19)

s_{\vec{\lambda}}=\big(P_{1},\ldots,P_{n},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\eta}\big),

Definition 4.12
.

The Beilinson-Drinfeld Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
of
\mathcal{G}
over
C^{n}
is defined to be the schematic image of
o_{\vec{\lambda}}:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}
, where
o_{\vec{\lambda}}
is defined as the following composition,

L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\simeq L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\times_{C^{n}}C^{n}\xrightarrow{{\rm Id}\times s_{\vec{\lambda}}}L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\times_{C^{n}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}\xrightarrow{\eqref{522693}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}.

When
n=1
,
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda}
is also called a global Schubert variety of
\mathcal{G}
.

Proposition 4.13
.

Given any
\vec{\lambda}\in(X_{*}(T)^{+})^{n}
and any partition
\xi=(I_{1},\cdots,I_{k})
of
[n]
, the BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
has a factorization property over
C^{n}_{\xi}
, i.e.

(4.20)

\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{k}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{j}}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{j}}}\bigg)\times_{C^{n}}C^{n}_{\xi},

where
\vec{\lambda}_{I_{j}}
is defined in (
3.22
).

Proof.

It suffices to prove the case when
\xi=(I,J)
. The map (
4.12
) can be given explicitly as follows:

(4.21)

\displaystyle\Phi:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\simeq\big(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{J}}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi}},

\displaystyle(\vec{p},\mathcal{F},\beta)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big((\vec{p}_{I},\mathcal{F}_{I},\beta_{I}),(\vec{p}_{J},\mathcal{F}_{J},\beta_{J})\big)

where by Beauville-Laszlo’s Lemma, there exists a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}_{I}
with isomorphisms
\delta_{I}:\mathcal{F}_{I}|_{\widehat{\cup_{i\in I}\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{\widehat{\cup_{i\in I}\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}}
and
\beta_{I}:\mathcal{F}_{I}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus{\cup_{i\in I}\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}}\to\mathring{\mathcal{F}}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus{\cup_{i\in I}\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}}
such that
\beta_{I}\circ\delta_{I}^{-1}|_{\widehat{\cup_{i\in I}\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}^{*}}=\beta|_{\widehat{\cup_{i\in I}\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}^{*}}
;
\mathcal{F}_{J},\beta_{J}
are defined similarly.

Let
s_{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}:C^{I}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}
(resp.
s_{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}
) be the section defined by (
4.19
). We shall show there is an equality of sections over
C^{n}_{\xi}

(4.22)

\Phi\circ s_{\vec{\lambda}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}=s_{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}\times s_{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}.

Recall the definition (
4.19
) of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}:=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{F}\times^{T}G)^{\Gamma}
and
\bar{\eta}
, where
\mathcal{F}
is a
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor defined via the functor
F
in (
4.15
), and
\eta
is the map induced from the natural maps
\eta_{\nu}
in (
4.18
).

Denote by
D_{I,\nu}
the divisor
\big(\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\big)\cap\big(C^{n}_{\xi}\times C\big)
in
C^{n}_{\xi}\times C
, and define
D_{J,\nu}
similarly. One may check that
D_{I,\nu}\cap D_{J,\nu}=\emptyset
for any
\nu
. Let
U_{I}\subseteq C^{n}_{\xi}\times C
be a neighborhood of
D_{I}:=\big(\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\Delta_{\gamma,i}\big)\cap\big(C^{n}_{\xi}\times C\big)
such that
U_{I}\cap D_{J,\nu}=\emptyset
for all
\nu
, and let
\mathrm{pr}_{I}:C^{n}_{\xi}\times C\to C^{I}\times C
be the projection map, then

\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\bigg)\Big|_{U_{I}}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{U_{I}}(D_{I,\nu})\simeq\mathrm{pr}_{I}^{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{I,\nu})\big)\Big)\big|_{U_{I}}.

By the definition of map (
4.21
), we have an equality of sections over
C^{n}_{\xi}

\Phi\circ s_{\vec{\lambda}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}=\big((\{P_{i}\}_{i\in I},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{I},\bar{\eta}_{I}),(\{P_{j}\}_{j\in J},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{J},\bar{\eta}_{J})\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi}},

where
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{I}=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{I}\times^{T}G)^{\Gamma}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor,
\mathcal{F}_{I}
is the
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor constructed via the functor
F_{I}:\mathrm{Rep}(T)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(C^{I}\times C)
given by

F_{I}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})=\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{I,\nu})\big)
,
and
\eta_{I}
is the trivialization induced from the natural maps
\eta_{I,\nu}:\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C\setminus\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{I})}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{I,\nu})\big)\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C\setminus\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{I})}
,
\nu\in X^{*}(T)
.
By definition (
4.19
),
(\{P_{i}\}_{j\in I},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{I},\bar{\eta}_{I})
is exactly the section
s_{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}:C^{I}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}
. Similarly,
(\{P_{j}\}_{i\in J},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{J},\bar{\eta}_{J})=s_{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}
. This shows that
\Phi\circ s_{\vec{\lambda}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}=s_{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}\times s_{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
. We also notice that the map
\Phi
is compatible with the factorization of
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
. It follows that the map (
4.21
) gives an isomorphism

\Phi:\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq\Big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{J}}\Big)\big|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
.
∎

4.3.
Convolution Grassmannian

With the same setup as in Section
4.2
, we define the convolution Grassmannian
\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
as follows: for each
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
R
,

\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}(R):=\left\{\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\vec{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\in C^{n}(R),\\
&\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{n}\text{ are }\mathcal{G}\text{-torsors on }\bar{C},\\
&\beta_{i}:\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{i-1}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}},\mathcal{F}_{0}:=\mathring{\mathcal{F}}.\end{aligned}\right\}.

There is a left
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-action on
\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
as follows. Given
(\vec{p},\zeta)\in L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}(R)
and
\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)\in\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}(R)
, by Beauville-Laszlo’s Lemma, there exists a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}
with isomorphisms
\delta_{i}:{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{i}|_{\hat{D}_{i}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{\hat{D}_{i}}
and
\alpha_{i}:\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus D_{i}}\simeq\mathring{\mathcal{F}}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus D_{i}}
such that
\alpha_{i}\circ\delta^{-1}_{i}|_{\hat{D}_{i}^{*}}=\zeta\circ\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{i}|_{\hat{D}_{i}^{*}}
, where
\hat{D}_{i}
is the formal completion of
\bar{C}_{R}
along the divisor
D_{i}:=\underset{\scriptscriptstyle 1\leq j\leq i}{\bigcup}\Delta_{\bar{p}_{j}}
and
\hat{D}_{i}^{*}:=\hat{D}_{i}\setminus D_{i}
. Thus, we get the following map

\displaystyle L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\times\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}

\displaystyle\Big((\vec{p},\zeta),\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{i},\beta^{\prime}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big),

where
\beta_{i}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\to\mathcal{F}_{i-1}^{\prime}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}
is given by

\beta_{i}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}\delta_{i-1}^{-1}\circ\beta_{i}\circ\delta_{i}|_{\hat{D}_{i-1}}\\
\alpha_{i-1}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{i}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus D_{i}}\end{cases}
.

Moreover, there is a convolution map

(4.23)

\mathfrak{m}:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}},

sending
\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)
to
\big(\vec{p},\mathcal{F}_{n},\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{n}\big)
. One can check that this map is
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant. Let
\xi_{0}=(\{1\},\{2\},\ldots,\{n\})
be the finest partition of
[n]
. We have the following isomorphism over
C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}=\{\vec{p}\in C^{n}\mid\bar{p}_{i}\neq\bar{p}_{j},\ \forall i\neq j\}
,

(4.24)

\varphi_{n}:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C}^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\big(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}},

sending
\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)
to
\big((p_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{i}}},\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{i}|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{i}}})_{i=1}^{n}\big)
.

Following
[
39
, (3.1.22)]
, we define an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C}
-torsor
\mathbb{E}
over
\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n-1}}\times C
by

\mathbb{E}(R):=\left\{\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1},\alpha\big)\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &\vec{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\in C^{n}(R),\\
&\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\text{ are }\mathcal{G}\text{-torsors on }\bar{C},\\
&\beta_{i}:\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{i-1}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{i}}},\mathcal{F}_{0}:=\mathring{\mathcal{F}},\\
&\alpha:\mathcal{F}_{n-1}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{n}}}\to\mathring{\mathcal{F}}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{n}}}.\end{aligned}\right\}.

Then, there is an isomorphism

(4.25)

\displaystyle f:\mathbb{E}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}},

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1},\alpha\big),(\mathcal{F},\beta)\Big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)

where by Beauville-Laszlo’s Lemma, there exists a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}_{n}
with isomorphisms
\beta_{n}:\mathcal{F}_{n}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{n}}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{n-1}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{n}}}
and
\delta:\mathcal{F}_{n}|_{\Delta_{\bar{p}_{n}}}\simeq\mathcal{F}|_{\Delta_{\bar{p}_{n}}}
such that
\beta_{n}\circ\delta^{-1}|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{n}}}=\alpha^{-1}\circ\beta|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{n}}}
. Composing
f
with the map (
4.24
), we get an isomorphism over
C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}

(4.26)

\displaystyle(\varphi\circ f)|_{C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}:\big(\mathbb{E}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\big(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1},\alpha\big),(\mathcal{F},\beta)\Big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big(\big(p_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{i}}},\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{i}|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\big)_{i=1}^{n}\Big).

By the constructions of
\mathcal{F}_{n}
and
\beta_{n}
above, we have

(p_{n},\mathcal{F}_{n}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{n}}},\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{n}|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{n}}})=(p_{n},\mathcal{F}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{n}}},\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{n-1}\circ\alpha^{-1}\circ\beta|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{n}}}).

Combining (
4.26
) and (
4.24
), we get an isomorphism over
C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}

(4.27)

\displaystyle\psi:\big(\mathbb{E}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\big(\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n-1}}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1},\alpha\big),(\mathcal{F},\beta)\Big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\Big(\big((p_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1}\big),(p_{n},\mathcal{F}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{n}}},\zeta\circ\beta|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{n}}})\Big),

where
\zeta=(\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{n-1})|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{n}}}\circ\alpha^{-1}\in L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{p_{n}}
.

In summary, we have the following commutative diagram

(4.28)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 57.86053pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-48.76814pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Definition 4.14
.

The convolution Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
is defined inductively as follows: when
n=1
, set
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda}:=\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda}
; when
n\geq 2
, define

\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}:=\mathbb{E}|_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n-1}}^{(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1})}\times C}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C}}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda_{n}}.

Now we would like to give another construction of the convolution Schubert variety. For each character
\nu
of
T
, we define

F_{i}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu}):=\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{i}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{k}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,k}\bigg).

This assignment extends to a
\mathbb{C}
-linear tensor functor
F_{i}:\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(C^{n+1})
. By the same argument as in Section
4.2
,
F_{i}
gives a locally trivial
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor
\mathcal{F}_{i}
over
C^{n+1}
. Moreover, the natural maps
\beta_{\nu,i}:F_{i}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})|_{C^{n}\times\bar{C}-\Delta_{\bar{P}_{i}}}\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}F_{i-1}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})|_{C^{n}\times\bar{C}-\Delta_{\bar{P}_{i}}}
for all
\nu\in X_{*}(T)
induce an isomorhism
\beta_{i}:\mathcal{F}_{i}|_{C^{n}\times\bar{C}-\Delta_{\bar{P}_{i}}}\to\mathcal{F}_{i-1}|_{C^{n}\times\bar{C}-\Delta_{\bar{P}_{i}}}
.

Set
\mathcal{F}_{i,G}=\mathcal{F}_{i}\times^{T}G
. Then
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{i}:=\pi_{*}(F_{i,G})^{\Gamma}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor over
C^{n}\times\bar{C}
, and
\beta_{i}
induces an isomorohism
\bar{\beta}_{i}:\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{i}|_{C^{n}\times\bar{C}-\Delta_{\bar{P}_{i}}}\simeq\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{i-1}|_{C^{n}\times\bar{C}-\Delta_{\bar{P}_{i}}}
, where by convention
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
is the trivial torsor. We construct a section
\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}}:C^{n}\to\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
as follows:

(4.29)

\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}}=\big(P_{1},\ldots,P_{n},(\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{i},\bar{\beta}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big).

We similary define a morphism
\tilde{o}_{\vec{\lambda}}:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
as in Definition
4.19
. Denote by
\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{C^{n}}
the schematic image of
\tilde{o}_{\vec{\lambda}}
.

Lemma 4.15
.

For any partition
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
of
[n]
, there is an isomorphism
\Phi:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}\to\big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{i}}}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
satisfying the following commutative diagram

(4.30)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 48.79291pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.42961pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.70836pt\raise-16.18056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.00002pt\raise-16.18056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{1024}\kern-1.78072pt\kern 1.78072pt}}}\kern
