51.7547pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 97.1562pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 97.1562pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 107.39032pt\raise-16.18056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{1024}\kern-1.78072pt\kern 1.78072pt}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.07886pt\raise-16.18056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.57501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{447893}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.07886pt\raise-22.91666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-48.79291pt\raise-32.36111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{i}}}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.2026pt\raise-24.35413pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.9931pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\prod\mathfrak{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.79291pt\raise-32.36111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 72.79291pt\raise-32.36111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{i}}}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Moreover, we have
\Phi\circ\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}=\prod\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{i}}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
. It follows that
\Phi
induces an isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{i}}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{i}}}\Big)\big|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}.

Proof.

We first define the map
\Phi
as follows,

(4.31)

\displaystyle\Phi:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\to\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{i}}}\Big)\big|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}

\displaystyle\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto{\textstyle\prod}\big(\vec{p}_{I_{i}},(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{j},\beta^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in I_{i}}\big)

where by Beauville-Laszlo’s Lemma, for any
j\in I_{i}
and the index
j^{\prime}
right before
j
in the ordered set
I_{i}
, there exists a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{j}
with isomorphisms
\alpha_{j}:\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{j}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{j}}}\to\mathcal{F}_{j}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{j}}}
and
\beta_{j}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{j}}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{j}}}\to{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}}}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\Delta_{\bar{p}_{j}}}
such that
\beta^{\prime}_{j}\circ\alpha^{-1}_{j}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{j}}^{*}}={\beta^{\prime}}_{j^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{j}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{j}}^{*}}
. One may check this is an isomorphism and satisfies the commutative diagram (
4.30
).

Let
\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}:C^{I}\to\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}
be the section defined by (
4.29
), we shall show
\textstyle\Phi\circ\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}=\prod\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{i}}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
over
C^{n}_{\xi}
for any partition
\xi
.
For the simplicity of illustration, we will only prove a special case for
n=4
and
\xi=(I,J)=(\{1,3\},\{2,4\})
, which indicates the argument in general. Recall the definition (
4.29
) of
\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{i}:=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{i}\times^{T}G)^{\Gamma}
and
\bar{\eta}_{i}
, where
\mathcal{F}_{i}
is a
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor defined via the functor
F_{i}:\mathrm{Rep}(T)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(C^{n+1})
given by

F_{i}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})=\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{i}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{j}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,j}\bigg),

and
\eta_{i}
is the map induced from the natural isomorphisms
\eta_{\nu,i}:F(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})|_{C^{n}\times C\setminus\bigcup_{j\leq i}\Delta_{\gamma,j}}\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}F^{0}(\mathbb{C}_{\nu})|_{C^{n}\times C\setminus\bigcup_{j\leq i}\Delta_{\gamma,j}}
,
\nu\in X^{*}(T)
. Denote by
D_{i,\nu}
the divisor
\big(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\big)\cap\big(C^{n}_{\xi}\times C\big)
in
C^{n}_{\xi}\times C
. One may check that
D_{i,\nu}\cap D_{j,\nu}=\emptyset
for any
i=1,3
,
j=2,4
. Let
U_{I}\subseteq C^{n}_{\xi}\times C
be a neighborhood of
\big(\bigcup_{i\in I,\gamma\in\Gamma}\Delta_{\gamma,i}\big)\cap\big(C^{n}_{\xi}\times C\big)
such that
U_{I}\cap(D_{2,\nu}\cup D_{4,\nu})=\emptyset
for any
\nu
, and let
\mathrm{pr}_{I}:C^{n}_{\xi}\times C\to C^{I}\times C
be the projection map. Define
U_{J}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{J}
similarly. Then

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{1}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,1}\bigg)\Big|_{U_{I}}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{U_{I}}(D_{1,\nu})\simeq\mathrm{pr}_{I}^{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{1,\nu})\big)\Big)\big|_{U_{I}},

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{C^{n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\langle\gamma(\lambda_{i}),\nu\rangle\Delta_{\gamma,i}\bigg)\Big|_{U_{I}}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{U_{I}}(D_{1,\nu}\cup D_{3,\nu})\simeq\mathrm{pr}_{I}^{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{1,\nu}\cup D_{3,\nu})\big)\Big)\big|_{U_{I}}.

By the definition of the map (
4.31
), we have an equality of sections over
C^{n}_{\xi}

\Phi\circ\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}=\big((P_{1},P_{3},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{3}^{\prime},\bar{\eta}_{1},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{3}),(P_{2},P_{4},\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2},\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{4},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{2},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{4})\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi}},

where
\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{3}=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{3}\times^{T}G)^{\Gamma}
, and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{3}
is the
(\Gamma,T)
-torsor constructed via the following functor
F^{\prime}_{3}:\mathrm{Rep}(T)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(C^{I}\times C)

F^{\prime}_{3}:\mathbb{C}_{\nu}\mapsto\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{1,\nu}\cup D_{3,\nu})\big),

\eta^{\prime}_{3}
is the map induced form the natural maps

\eta^{\prime}_{3,\nu}:\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C\setminus\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{3,\nu})}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{1,\nu}\cup D_{3,\nu})\big)\to\mathscr{O}_{C^{I}\times C\setminus\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{3})}(\mathrm{pr}_{I}(D_{1,\nu})),

for all
\nu\in X^{*}(T)
.
By definition (
4.29
),
(P_{1},P_{3},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\bar{\mathcal{F}}_{3}^{\prime},\bar{\eta}_{1},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{3})
is exactly the section
\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}:C^{I}\to\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}
. Similarly,
(P_{2},P_{4},\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2},\bar{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{4},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{2},\bar{\eta}^{\prime}_{4})=\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}
. This shows that

\Phi\circ\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}=\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}\times\tilde{s}_{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
.
Since
\Phi
is
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
-equivariant, it follows that the map (
4.31
) induces an isomorphism
\Phi:\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq\big(\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}\times\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{J}}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
.
∎

Recall that there is an isomorphism (
4.24
) over
C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}
:

\varphi_{n}:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}\to\big(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C_{\xi_{0}}^{n}}.

By Lemma
4.15
, this map
\varphi
induces an isomorphism

(4.32)

\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}\simeq\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{n}}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}.

Proposition 4.16
.

The morphism
f
defined in (
4.25
) induces an isomorphism

(4.33)

\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{C^{n}}.

Proof.

We prove it by induction on
n
. When
n=1
, by definition
\tilde{s}_{\lambda}=s_{\lambda}
. Hence,
\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},C}=\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda}=\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda}
. When
n\geq 2
, by induction we have
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n-1}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
. Then, the map
\psi
in (
4.27
) induces an isomorphism

(4.34)

\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\big(\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda_{n}}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}.

Combining with the isomorphism (
4.32
), we get the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 51.23715pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.9568pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.91678pt\raise-17.60056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25555pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{342960}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.00002pt\raise-17.60056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{1024}\kern-1.78072pt\kern 1.78072pt}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0722pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 120.11934pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.66539pt\raise-17.60056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{1024}\kern-1.78072pt\kern 1.78072pt}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.35393pt\raise-17.60056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25555pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{234926}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 137.35393pt\raise-25.70113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-51.23715pt\raise-35.20113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big(\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda_{n}}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.11215pt\raise-30.85684pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.81346pt\raise-41.04834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.01389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{234926}\times\mathrm{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.23715pt\raise-35.20113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.23715pt\raise-35.20113pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{n}}_{\mathcal{G},C}\big)|_{C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

This gives an isomorphism
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi_{0}}}
. By the commutativity of the diagram (
4.28
), this isomorphism is exactly the restriction of the map
f
defined in (
4.25
). Since both of them are reduced and irreducible, we must have an isomorphism
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
.
∎

4.4.
Flatness of BD Schubert varieties

Let
G
be a simple algebraic group of adjoint type over
\mathbb{C}
, and let
\sigma
be the standard automorphism defined in Section
2.2
. Let
\mathcal{K}=\mathbb{C}((t))
and
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{C}[[t]]
. We define the action of
\sigma
on
\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{O}
by
\sigma(t)=\epsilon^{-1}t
, where
\epsilon
is a fixed primitive
m
-th root of unity. Denote by
\bar{\mathcal{K}}=\mathcal{K^{\sigma}}
and
\bar{\mathcal{O}}=\mathcal{O}^{\sigma}
the
\sigma
-fixed points in
\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{O}
respectively.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be the
\sigma
-fixed point subgroup scheme
\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{O}/\bar{\mathcal{O}}}(G_{\mathcal{O}})^{\sigma}
of the Weil restriction
\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{O}/\bar{\mathcal{O}}}(G_{\mathcal{O}})
. Then
\mathscr{G}
is a special parahoric group scheme over
\bar{\mathcal{O}}
in the sense of Bruhat-Tits, see
[
4
, Section 2.2]
. Let
L\mathscr{G}
be the loop group scheme and
L^{+}\mathscr{G}
be the jet group scheme with
L\mathscr{G}(R)=\mathscr{G}(R((t)))
and
L^{+}\mathscr{G}(R)=\mathscr{G}(R[[t]])
, for any
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
R
. We define the affine grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}
to be the fppf quotient
L\mathscr{G}/L^{+}\mathscr{G}
, and call it a twisted affine Grassmannian of
\mathscr{G}
. Similarly, replacing
\mathscr{G}
by
G
, we define the affine Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}
of
G
to be the fppf quotient
LG/L^{+}G
. In particular,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathcal{K})^{\sigma}/G(\mathcal{O})^{\sigma}
and
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}(\mathbb{C})=G(\mathcal{K})/G(\mathcal{O})
. We denote by
e_{0}
the base point in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}(\mathbb{C})
.

For any dominant coweight
\lambda\in X_{*}(T)
, we are associated to an element
t^{\lambda}\in T(\mathcal{K})
. Then, the closure of the orbit
G(\mathcal{O})\cdot t^{\lambda}e_{0}
in
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}(\mathbb{C})
is called an affine Schubert variety, denoted by
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda}
. Let
n^{\lambda}
be the norm of
t^{\lambda}
, i.e.
n^{\lambda}:=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}\sigma^{i}(t^{\lambda})\in T(\mathcal{K})^{\sigma}
. Let
\bar{\lambda}\in X_{*}(T)_{\sigma}
be the image of
\lambda
under the projection map
X_{*}(T)\to X_{*}(T)_{\sigma}
, and denote by
e_{\bar{\lambda}}
the point
n^{\lambda}e_{0}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathbb{C})
. One can define a Schubert cell
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\bar{\lambda}}:=G(\mathcal{O})^{\sigma}e_{\bar{\lambda}}
. We define the Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\bar{\lambda}}
to be the reduced closure of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\bar{\lambda}}
in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}
. The dimensions of
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda}
and
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\bar{\lambda}}
are given by

(4.35)

\dim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda}=\dim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\bar{\lambda}}=2\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle,

where
\rho
is the summation of fundamental weights of
G
, cf.
[
39
, Proposition 2.1.5]
[
4
, Sectioin 2.3]
. Moreover, there is a level one line bundle
\mathscr{L}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}
such that

(4.36)

H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\bar{\lambda}},\mathscr{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\simeq D^{\sigma}(c,\bar{\lambda}),

as
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-modules, where
D^{\sigma}(c,\bar{\lambda})
is the twisted affine Demazure module defined in Section
2.4
, cf.
[
4
, Theorem 3.11]
).

Let
\Gamma
be the cyclic group generated by
\sigma
, and let
C
be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action. From now on, we assume that the ramified points of
C
are totally ramified, i.e. the stabilizer
\Gamma_{p}
is equal to
\Gamma
for any ramified point
p
. Moreover, when
C
is the affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, we require the
\Gamma
-action to be the ‘standard’ one given by

(4.37)

\sigma(p)=\epsilon p,\quad\text{ for any }p\in\mathbb{A}^{1}.

Theorem 4.17
.

With the assumptions above, the global Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},C}
is flat over
C
, and for any
p\in C
the fiber
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},p}
is reduced. Moreover,

(4.38)

\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},p}\simeq\begin{cases}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathscr{G}}^{\bar{\lambda}}&\text{for }p\in R;\\
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda}&\text{for }p\not\in R,\end{cases}

where
R
denotes the ramification locus of the action of
\Gamma
on
C
.

Proof.

When
C=\mathbb{A}^{1}
, the theorem was proven in
[
38
, Theorem 3]
. Now, let
C
be a general
\Gamma
-curve whose points are either unramified or totally ramified. Note that the theorem is local, we shall reduce it to the affine case. For any ramified point
x\in C
, there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
x
with a
\Gamma
-equivariant étale map
U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
sending
x
to
0
.
By Corollary
4.8
, we have a base change isomorphism

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{U}},U}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}U,

and the left
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{U}
-action on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{U}},U}
commutes with the base change. Thus,

\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{U}},U}^{\lambda}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}U.

Hence, the theorem holds over
U
. Similarly, one can show that the theorem also holds over
C\backslash R
. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Lemma 4.18
.

The convolution variety
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is flat over
C^{n}
. Moreover, the convolution morphism
\mathfrak{m}
induces a surjective morphism
\mathfrak{m}:\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
.

Proof.

Recall the Definition
4.14
that
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is a global Schubert variety when
n=1
and is a sequence of fibrations of global Schubert varieties when
n\geq 2
. By Theorem
4.17
,
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
must be flat over
C^{n}
.

By Proposition
4.16
,
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
can be identified with the schematic image of the map
\tilde{o}_{\vec{\lambda}}:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
. By the definition (
4.23
) of convolution map
\mathfrak{m}
and the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariance of
\mathfrak{m}
, we have a commutative diagram

(4.39)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.46085pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.46085pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.45497pt\raise-22.82248pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.85971pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{o_{\vec{\lambda}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.29285pt\raise-24.56447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.16397pt\raise 8.46112pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.76112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{o}_{\vec{\lambda}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.46085pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.46085pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.89046pt\raise-16.57585pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.89046pt\raise-24.56447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.89778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 48.9678pt\raise-31.89778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Since
\mathfrak{m}
is proper, it gives a surjective map
\mathfrak{m}:\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
.
∎

We define the following subset in
C^{n}
:

(4.40)

\Delta_{[n]}=\{\vec{p}\in C^{n}\mid\bar{p}_{i}=\bar{p}_{j},\ \forall 1\leq i,j\leq n\}.

Proposition 4.19
.

With the same assumptions in Theorem
4.17
, we have

(1)

For any point
\vec{p}=(p,\gamma_{2}(p),\ldots,\gamma_{n}(p))\in\Delta_{[n]}
with
\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma
, we have

\Big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\Big)_{\mathrm{red}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p}^{\lambda_{\vec{p}}},

where
\lambda_{\vec{p}}:=\lambda_{1}+\gamma_{2}^{-1}(\lambda_{2})+\cdots+\gamma_{n}^{-1}(\lambda_{n})
.

(2)

For any point
\vec{p}\in C^{n}
, we have

R^{i}(\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{p}})_{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}}\Big)=\begin{cases}\mathscr{O}_{\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\big)_{\mathrm{red}}},&i=0;\\
0,&\forall i\geq 1.\end{cases}

Proof.

(1) Taking the reduced fibers of both sides of the morphism
\mathfrak{m}:\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\twoheadrightarrow\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
in Lemma
4.18
, we get a surjective map for any
\vec{p}\in C^{n}
,

\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{p}}:\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}=\Big(\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}\Big)_{\mathrm{red}}\twoheadrightarrow\Big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}\Big)_{\mathrm{red}}.

Now, let
\vec{p}=(p,\gamma_{2}(p),\ldots,\gamma_{n}(p))
be any point in
\Delta_{[n]}
. To prove part (1), it suffices to show the image of
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}
under the convolution map
\mathfrak{m}:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
is
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{\vec{p}}}_{\mathcal{G},p}
. When
p\in C
is unramified, the composition of morphisms (
4.23
) (
4.25
) restricts to the following map:

(4.41)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|_{\vec{p}}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\gamma_{n}(p)}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\gamma_{n}(p)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1.5em]{\sim}}\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1.5em]{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n-1},\alpha\big),(\mathcal{F},\beta)\Big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\vec{p},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\big)\mapsto(\vec{p},\mathcal{F}|_{\hat{\Delta}_{\bar{p}_{n}}},\zeta\circ\beta|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{n}}})

where
p_{n}:=\gamma_{n}(p)
,
\zeta=(\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{n-1}\circ\alpha^{-1})|_{\hat{\Delta}^{*}_{\bar{p}_{n}}}\in G(\mathcal{K}_{p_{n}})
. It factors through the usual convolution map

G(\mathcal{K}_{p_{n}})\times^{G(\mathcal{O}_{p_{n}})}\mathrm{Gr}_{G,p_{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{G,p_{n}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}.

By induction on
n
, for
\vec{q}:=(p,\gamma_{2}(p),\cdots,\gamma_{n-1}(p))\in C^{n-1}
, the image of
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{q}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}
under the convolution map is
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{\vec{q}}}_{\mathcal{G},p}
. Thus for any
(\vec{q},(\mathcal{F}_{i},\beta_{i})_{i})\in\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{q}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}
, we have
(p,\mathcal{F}_{n-1},\beta_{1}\circ\cdots\circ\beta_{n-1})\in\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G,p}^{\lambda_{\vec{q}}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G,p_{n}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})}
. Therefore, the following restriction of (
4.41
)

\mathbb{E}|_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{q}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}},p_{n}}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{p_{n}}}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{n}}_{G,p_{n}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})+\lambda_{n}}_{G,p_{n}},

factors through
\mathfrak{m}_{p_{n}}:\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p_{n}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{p_{n}}}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{n}}_{G,p_{n}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})+\lambda_{n}}_{G,p_{n}}
, where
\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p_{n}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})}
is the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{p}
-torsor over
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})}_{G,p_{n}}
. This implies that the image of
\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}
under the convolution map is
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})+\lambda_{n}}_{G,p_{n}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{\vec{p}}}_{G,p}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{\vec{p}}}_{\mathcal{G},p}
. The case when
p\in C
is totally ramified can be proved similarly.

For part (2), by Lemma
4.15
it suffices to consider the case when
\vec{p}\in\Delta_{[n]}
. We now the consider the morphism

\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{p}}:\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
. From part (1) and
[
31
, Proposition 9.7]
, we know
(\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{p}})_{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}}\Big)=\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{\vec{p}}}_{\mathcal{G},p}}=\mathscr{O}_{\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\big)_{\mathrm{red}}}
.

For
i\geq 1
, we shall show
R^{i}(\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{p}})_{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}}\Big)=0
by induction on
n
. Clearly, this holds when
n=1
. Now, assume
n\geq 2
, and let
\vec{q}=(p,\gamma_{2}(p),\ldots,\gamma_{n-1}(p))\in C^{n-1}
. By induction,
R^{i}(\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{q}})_{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{q}}^{\vec{\lambda}}}\Big)=0
.
Consider the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 50.76398pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-50.76398pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{E}|_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{q}}\times\{p_{n}\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.59377pt\raise-17.57492pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.76112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{\vec{q}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.97905pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.99313pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.76398pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.76398pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.55635pt\raise-17.57492pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.85971pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{q}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.55635pt\raise-23.97905pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-12.31467pt\raise-35.14986pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p}^{\lambda_{\vec{q}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.50732pt\raise-29.96236pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.51945pt\raise-35.14986pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 86.51945pt\raise-35.14986pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{\vec{q}}}_{\mathcal{G},p}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

By flat base change
[
21
, Proposition 9.3]
, we have
R^{i}(\tilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{\vec{q}})_{*}\big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}}\big)=0
for
i\geq 1
.
From part (1), there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}\simeq\mathbb{E}|_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{q}}^{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n-1}}\times\{p_{n}\}}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{p_{n}}}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{n}}_{\mathcal{G},p_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{p}}}
\textstyle{W:=\widetilde{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p_{n}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})}\times^{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{p_{n}}}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{n}}_{\mathcal{G},p_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{p_{n}}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\gamma_{n}(\lambda_{\vec{q}})+\lambda_{n}}_{\mathcal{G},p_{n}}.}

Thus,
R^{i}f_{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}}\Big)=0
. Recall that
m_{p_{n}}
is a partial Bott-Samelson resolution and we have
R^{i}({\mathfrak{m}_{p_{n}}})_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{W})=0
, cf.
[
31
, Proposition 9.7]
. Thus,
R^{i}(\mathfrak{m}_{\vec{p}})_{*}\Big(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}}\Big)=0
.
∎

Theorem 4.20
.

With the same assumptions as in Theorem
4.17
, the BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is flat over
C^{n}
. Moreover,
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is reduced.

Proof.

Let
X=\overline{\mathrm{Conv}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
and
Y=\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
. By Lemma
4.18
and Proposition
4.19
, we can apply Corollary
B.4
to the morphism
\mathfrak{m}:X\to Y
and we get
\mathfrak{m}_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is flat over
C^{n}
. To prove the first part of the theorem, it suffices to show
\mathfrak{m}_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})\simeq\mathscr{O}_{Y}
. Consider the natural map
\mathscr{O}_{Y}\to\mathfrak{m}_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
. At every point
s=\vec{p}\in C^{n}
, the composition of the following maps

(4.42)

\mathscr{O}_{Y_{s}}\to\mathfrak{m}_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})|_{Y_{s}}\xrightarrow[\text{part (1) of Corollary \ref{173291}}]{\sim}(\mathfrak{m}_{s})_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{s}})\xrightarrow[\text{Proposition \ref{930915}}]{\sim}\mathscr{O}_{(Y_{s})_{\mathrm{red}}}

is surjective. Thus, the morphism
\mathscr{O}_{Y}\to\mathfrak{m}_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is surjective.

Recall that
Y
is the schematic image of
o_{\vec{\lambda}}:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
. By Proposition
4.16
,
X
is the schematic image of
\tilde{o}_{\vec{\lambda}}:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
.
Thus, we have an inclusion
\mathscr{O}_{X}\hookrightarrow(\tilde{o}_{\vec{\lambda}})_{*}\big(\mathscr{O}_{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}}\big)
. By Proposition
4.19
, we have
R^{1}\mathfrak{m}_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})=0
. It follows that we have the following inclusion

\mathfrak{m}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X}\hookrightarrow\big(\mathfrak{m}_{*}\circ(\tilde{o}_{\vec{\lambda}})_{*}\big)\big(\mathscr{O}_{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}}\big)=(o_{\vec{\lambda}})_{*}\big(\mathscr{O}_{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}}\big).

From the commutativity of the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.15524pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.15524pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.15526pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.15524pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.15524pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(o_{\vec{\lambda}})_{*}\big(\mathscr{O}_{L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}}\big)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-10.35037pt\raise-32.41666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{O}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-7.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 1.99997pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.35039pt\raise-28.17139pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.6297pt\raise-9.59282pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 76.01219pt\raise-32.41666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

we must have
\mathfrak{m}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{Y}
. Thus,
\mathscr{O}_{Y}
is flat over
C^{n}
. On the other hand, this also implies that the composition map (
4.42
) is an isomorphism. In particular, it follows that
Y_{s}=\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is reduced.
∎

Corollary 4.21
.

With the same assumptions in Theorem
4.17
, we have

(1)

Let
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
be a partition of
[n]
. For any
\vec{p}\in C^{n}_{\xi}
, we have

\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}_{I_{1}}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{1}}}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}_{I_{k}}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{k}}},

where
\vec{p}_{I_{j}}\in C^{I_{j}}
and
\vec{\lambda}_{I_{j}}
are defined in (
3.21
) and (
3.22
).

(2)

For any
\vec{p}=(p,\gamma_{2}(p),\ldots,\gamma_{n}(p))\in\Delta_{[n]}
, we have
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p}^{\lambda_{\vec{p}}}
,
where
\lambda_{\vec{p}}:=\lambda_{1}+\gamma_{2}^{-1}(\lambda_{2})+\cdots+\gamma_{n}^{-1}(\lambda_{n})
.

Proof.

Part (1) directly follows from Proposition
4.13
. Part (2) follows from Proposition
4.19
and Theorem
4.20
.
∎

Lemma 4.22
.

The orbit
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}:=L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\cdot o^{\vec{\lambda}}
is smooth over
C^{n}
.

Proof.

​Observe that
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is open in
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
. By Theorem
4.20
,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is also flat over
C^{n}
. Since every fiber
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is smooth,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
is smooth over
C^{n}
, cf.
[
21
, Theorem 10.2]
.
∎

Theorem 4.23
.

The BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
is normal.

Proof.

When
n=1
, the theorem was proved in
[
38
, Section 3.3]
. For
n\geq 2
, we prove it by induction on
n
. Given a non-trivial partition
\xi=(I,J)
of
[n]
, by Proposition
4.13
there is a factorization over
C^{n}_{\xi}

\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq\Big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{J}}\Big)\big|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}.

By induction,
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}
and
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{J}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{J}}
are normal, hence
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}
is normal. Note that the complement of the union of
C^{n}_{\xi}
for all non-trivial partition
(I,J)
in
C^{n}
is
\Delta_{[n]}
. To show the normality around
\Delta_{[n]}
, in view of Corollary
4.8
we can assume
C=\mathbb{A}^{1}
. For the rest of proof, we adapt an argument in
[
38
, Proposition 6.4]
.

Let
X=\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. For any
i\in I
,
j\in J
and
0\leq k\leq m-1
, we regard
t:=z_{i}-\sigma^{k}(z_{j})
as a regular function on
X
. We shall show the local ring
\mathscr{O}_{X,x}
is normal for any
x\in X
such that
t(x)=0
. Denote by
(X_{0})_{\mathrm{red}}
the reduced fiber of
t:X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
at
0\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
. By Corollary
4.21
,
(X_{0})_{\mathrm{red}}
is isomorphic to a BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\mu}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n-1}}
, where
\vec{\mu}
consists of
\lambda_{a}
,
1\leq a\leq n
with
a\not=i,j
, and
\lambda_{i}+\sigma^{k}(\lambda_{j})
. Thus,
t\mathscr{O}_{X,x}
has a unique minimal associated prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
. By induction,
(X_{0})_{\mathrm{red}}
is normal, hence
\mathscr{O}_{X,x}/\mathfrak{p}
is normal. By the dimension formula (
4.35
), the complement of the orbit
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C^{n}}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
in
X
has codimension greater than or equal to
2
. Applying Lemma
4.22
,
X
is regular in codimension one, which implies that the localization
(\mathscr{O}_{X,x})_{\mathfrak{p}}
at the minimal ideal
\mathfrak{p}
is regular. Thus,
\mathscr{O}_{X,x}
is normal, since the three conditions of
[
21
, Lemma 9.12]
are satisfied. Since
i\in I
,
j\in J
and
0\leq k\leq m-1
are arbitrary, we conclude that
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}=X
is normal.
∎

We conclude this subsection by showing that
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
is a direct limit of
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
with respect to the standard partial order
\preceq
on
(X_{*}(T)^{+})^{n}
. The following Theorem is due to Wyatt Reeves
[
36
]
.

Theorem 4.24
.

Let
S
be a Noetherian scheme. Let
X\to Y
be an ind-closed embedding of ind-schemes over
S
. Suppose
X/S
is ind-flat and
Y/S
is ind-finite-type. Suppose
S
admits a decomposition into a closed subscheme
Z
and its open complement
U=S\setminus Z
such that
X|_{Z}\to Y|_{Z}
and
X|_{U}\to Y|_{U}
are isomorphisms. Then
X\to Y
is an isomorphism. ∎

The following result was proved in
[
36
]
in the untwisted case as a consequence of Theorem
4.24
. The same method can be adapted in our twisted setting.

Proposition 4.25
.

The BD Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
is a direct limit of
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}
with respect to the standard partial order
\preceq
on
(X_{*}(T)^{+})^{n}
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
n
. When
n=1
, consider the ind-closed embedding

(4.43)

\varinjlim_{\lambda}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}.

Let
R
be the set of ramified points in
C
. Set
\mathring{C}=C\backslash R
. By Corollary
4.8
(2), we have an isomorphism
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathring{C}}
. This isomorphism restricts to an isomorphism
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{G,\mathring{C}}
. By
[
36
]
,
\varinjlim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G,\mathring{C}}^{\lambda}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathring{C}}
is an isomorphism. Thus,
\varinjlim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}^{\lambda}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}
is also an isomorphism. For each
p\in R
,
\varinjlim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p}^{\lambda}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},p}
is an isomorphism, cf.
[
31
, Proposition 9.9]
. Repeatedly applying Theorem
4.24
at ramified points, we conclude that (
4.43
) is an isomorphism.

When
n\geq 2
, consider the following ind-closed embedding

(4.44)

\varinjlim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}.

For any nontrivial partition
\xi=(I,J)
of
[n]
, by induction and the factorization maps (
4.12
) (
4.20
), the restriction of (
4.44
) to
C^{n}_{\xi}
is an isomorphism. Note that
\bigcup_{\xi}{C^{n}_{\xi}}=C^{n}\setminus\Gamma^{n}\cdot\Delta
, where
\Gamma^{n}
acts on the diagonal
\Delta
via the obvious action. We get an isomorphism

\varinjlim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}\setminus\Gamma^{n}\cdot\Delta}^{\vec{\lambda}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}\setminus\Gamma^{n}\cdot\Delta}.

Note that
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{\gamma}(\Delta)}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}
. Moreover, by Corollary
4.21
,
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{\gamma}(\Delta)}^{\vec{\lambda}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C}^{\lambda}
for any
\vec{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n})
,
\vec{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})
, and
\lambda=\sum\gamma_{i}^{-1}(\lambda_{i})
. By the case when
n=1
, (
4.43
) induces an isomorphism
\varinjlim\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{\gamma}(\Delta)}^{\vec{\lambda}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{\gamma}(\Delta)}
for any
\vec{\gamma}\in\Gamma^{n}
. Repeatedly applying Theorem
4.24
at
\vec{\gamma}(\Delta)
, we conclude that (
4.44
) is an isomorphism. ∎

Remark 4.26
.

All results in this subsection remain true for any algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p
, if
p\not\mid m
. Moreover, if
\Gamma
is trivial or generated by a diagram automorphism, all results still hold.

5.
Line bundles on Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian of
\mathcal{G}

In this section, we determine the rigidified Picard group of the BD Grassamnnian
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
of
\mathcal{G}
when
\mathcal{G}
is generically simply-connected, along the way we construct the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
. We also establish the factorizable and
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
. This allow us to construct factorizable and equivariant level one line bundles on BD Schubert varieties of
\mathcal{G}
when
C=\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
\mathcal{G}
is of adjoint type generically.

5.1.
Picard group of rigidified line bundles on BD Grassmannian of
\mathcal{G}

We first make a digression to prove a relative version of seesaw principal, which is originally due to Mumford
[
28
, Corollary 6]
.

Lemma 5.1

(Seesaw Theorem)
.

Let
\phi:X\to S
and
\psi:Y\to S^{\prime}
be projective and flat morphisms of varieties over
\mathbb{C}
such that, for any
s\in S
and
s^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}
, the schematic fibers
X_{s}
and
Y_{s^{\prime}}
are integral. Given an open subset
U\subset S\times S^{\prime}
, let
L
be a line bundle on
(X\times Y)|_{U}
such that the restriction
L|_{X_{s}\times q}
and
L|_{p\times Y_{s^{\prime}}}
are trivial for any
(s,q)\in(S\times Y)|_{U}
,
(p,s^{\prime})\in(X\times S^{\prime})|_{U}
. Then there is a line bundle
M
on
U
such that
L\simeq\pi^{*}M
, where
\pi=(\phi\times\psi)|_{U}:(X\times Y)|_{U}\to U
.

Proof.

Consider the morphism
\pi_{2}=(\phi\times\mathrm{id})|_{U}:(X\times Y)|_{U}\to(S\times Y)|_{U}
. By assumption, for any
(s,q)\in(S\times Y)|_{U}
, the line bundle
L|_{X_{s}\times q}
is trivial. Since
X
is projective over
S
, we have
\dim\big(H^{0}(X_{s}\times q,L|_{X_{s}\times q})\big)=1
for any
(s,q)\in(S\times Y)|_{U}
. It follows that
(\pi_{2})_{*}L
is a locally free sheaf of rank
1
over
(S\times Y)|_{U}
. Let
L^{\prime}=(\pi_{2})_{*}L
. By adjunction, we have a morphism
(\pi_{2})^{*}L^{\prime}\to L
. This is an isomorphism since the map
H^{0}(X_{s}\times q,L)\to L_{(p,q)}
is an isomorphism for any
(p,q)\in(X\times Y)|_{U}
.

Similarly, consider the morphism
\pi_{1}=(\mathrm{id}\times\psi)|_{U}:(S\times Y)|_{U}\to U
and note that
L^{\prime}|_{s\times Y_{s^{\prime}}}
is trivial for any
(s,s^{\prime})\in U
. By the same argument as above,
(\pi_{1})_{*}L^{\prime}
is a locally free sheaf of rank
1
over
U
, and
L^{\prime}\simeq(\pi_{1})^{*}M
where
M=(\pi_{1})_{*}L^{\prime}
. Therefore,

L\simeq(\pi_{2})^{*}L^{\prime}\simeq(\pi_{2})^{*}(\pi_{1})^{*}M\simeq\pi^{*}M.\qed

Let
\pi:X\to S
be a projective and flat morphism of varieties such that every fiber is integral. Given a section
e:S\to X
, let
(L,\alpha)
denote a rigidified line bundle on
X
, that is, a line bundle
L
with an isomorphism
\alpha:\mathscr{O}_{S}\simeq e^{*}L
. We denote by
\mathcal{P}\mathit{ic}^{e}(X)
the Picard groupoid of these rigidified line bundles on
X
with respect to
e
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\pi:X\to S
be a morphism as above. Let
(L,\alpha)
,
(L^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
be rigidified line bundles in
\mathcal{P}\mathit{ic}^{e}(X)
. If
\psi:L\simeq L^{\prime}
is an isomorphism of line bundles on
X
, then there exists a unique isomorphism
\phi:(L,\alpha)\simeq(L^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{P}\mathit{ic}^{e}(X)
, i.e. there is a unique isomorphism
\phi:L\simeq L^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes

(5.1)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 11.02953pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.02953pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{e^{*}L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.99982pt\raise 6.54709pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.79709pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{e^{*}(\phi)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.60039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.81496pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 78.60039pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{e^{*}L^{\prime}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.62003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 35.02953pt\raise-31.62003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{O}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.84918pt\raise-20.31696pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.86673pt\raise-5.55554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.72424pt\raise-21.70781pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.91098pt\raise-5.55554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 88.0327pt\raise-31.62003pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Proof.

By a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
, there exists an isomorphism
\pi^{*}\pi_{*}L\simeq L
. Applying
e^{*}
, we get
\pi_{*}L\simeq e^{*}L
. Thus,

(5.2)

{\rm Hom}(L,L)\simeq{\rm Hom}(\pi^{*}\pi_{*}L,L)\simeq{\rm Hom}(\pi_{*}L,\pi_{*}L)\simeq{\rm Hom}(e^{*}L,e^{*}L).

If there are two isomorphisms
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}:(L,\alpha)\simeq(L^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
, then
\phi_{2}^{-1}\circ\phi_{1}
is an automorphism of
(L,\alpha)\in\mathcal{P}\mathit{ic}^{e}(X)
. In view of the isomorphism (
5.2
), this must be an identity. This proves the uniqueness.

Given an isomorphism
\psi:L\simeq L^{\prime}
, set
\beta=e^{*}(\psi)\circ\alpha:\mathscr{O}_{S}\simeq e^{*}L^{\prime}
, which gives another rigidification of
L^{\prime}
. By the isomorphism (
5.2
), there exists a unique
\eta\in\mathrm{Aut}(L^{\prime})
such that
e^{*}(\eta)=\alpha^{\prime}\circ\beta^{-1}
. Then, the isomorphism
\phi:=\eta\circ\psi:L\to L^{\prime}
satisfies the commutative diagram (
5.1
). Hence, it gives an isomorphism
(L,\alpha)\simeq(L^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{P}\mathit{ic}^{e}(X)
.
∎

By Lemma
5.2
, the groupoid
\mathcal{P}\mathit{ic}^{e}(X)
is discrete. We will use this lemma to glue rigidified line bundles. Let
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(X)
denote the group of isomorphism classes in
\mathcal{P}\mathit{ic}^{e}(X)
, and call it the rigidified Picard group of
X
along
e
. Given an isomorphism class
[(\mathcal{L},\alpha)]\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(X)
, by abuse of notation, we simply denote it by
\mathcal{L}
.

From now on, we are in the setup of Section
4.4
. We further assume that the simple algebraic group
G
is simply-connected.

Let
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})
be the rigidified Picard group of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
along the section
e:C^{n}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
. Here, we abuse the notation
e
for the section
e_{\vec{0}}
over
C^{n}
. Given a rigidified line bundle
\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
, we denote by
\mathcal{L}|_{\Delta_{C^{n}}}
the restriction of
\mathcal{L}
to the BD Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\Delta_{C^{n}}}
over the diagonal.
By
[
38
, Proposition 4.1]
, the central charge of
\mathcal{L}|_{\vec{p}}
for any
\vec{p}\in\Delta_{C^{n}}
is constant. This gives a well-defined central charge map

(5.3)

c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})\to\mathbb{Z}.

We shall show the map
c
is an isomorphism.

We first deal with the case when
C=\mathbb{A}^{1}
with the standard
\Gamma
-action in (
4.37
). Recall that when
C=\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}
, where the positive generator will be denoted by
\mathcal{L}
. Consider the projection
\mathrm{pr}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}}\to\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}
. Let

(5.4)

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}:=(\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathcal{L})|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}}

denote the restriction of the line bundle
\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathcal{L}
to
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
belongs to
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}})
since
e^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
is a trivial bundle over
\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Note that the restriction
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}|_{\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}
to the diagonal, regarded as a line bundle over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}
, is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. Moreover, the restriction of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
to each fiber is the ample generator in
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},p})
for any
p\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
, cf.
[
4
, Corollary 3.14]
. Hence
c(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})=1
, which implies that
c
is a surjection. From now on, we call
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
the level one line bundle over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
C
be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action and assume that all ramified points in
C
are totally ramified. Then the central charge map (
5.3
) is an isomorphism

c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

Let
R:=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\}
be the set of ramified points in
C
. Set
\mathring{C}=C\backslash R
. By Corollary
4.8
,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathring{C}}
. Then, by
[
39
, Lemma 3.4.2]
, the following central charge map is an isomorphism

(5.5)

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

We consider the following restriction map

(5.6)

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\to\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}).

Then, the composition of (
5.6
) and (
5.5
) is the central charge map

(5.7)

c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\to\mathbb{Z}.

We first show the restriction map (
5.6
) is injective. Let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})
such that the restriction
\mathcal{L}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}}
is trivial. We shall show
\mathcal{L}
is also trivial. By Proposition
4.25
, it suffices to show the restriction of
\mathcal{L}
to
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},C}
is trivial for all dominant coweight
\lambda
. We still denote the restriction by
\mathcal{L}
. By assumption, there is an isomorphism
\theta:\mathscr{O}_{C}\simeq e^{*}\mathcal{L}
, and
\mathcal{L}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}}
is trivial. By Lemma
5.2
, there is a unique isomorphism

s:\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}^{\lambda}}\simeq\mathcal{L}|_{\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{C}}^{\lambda}}

such that
e^{*}(s)=\theta|_{\mathring{C}}
. Let
D_{i}
denote the divisor
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},p_{i}}^{\lambda}
. By Theorem
4.23
, there is a unique integer
n
such that

s\in\Gamma(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C\setminus\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{k-1}\}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}(nD_{k}))\setminus\Gamma(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C\setminus\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{k-1}\}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}((n-1)D_{k})).

Now, we regard
s
as a regular section of
\mathcal{L}(nD_{k})
. Then
e^{*}(s)=\theta|_{\mathring{C}}
can be regarded a section of
(e^{*}\mathcal{L})|_{\mathring{C}}
with pole order
n
at
p_{k}\in C
. Since
\theta
is regular at
p_{k}
, we must have

n=0
, and hence
s
induces an isomorphism

s:\mathscr{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C\setminus\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{k-1}\}}^{\lambda}}\simeq\mathcal{L}|_{\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},C\setminus\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{k-1}\}}^{\lambda}}.

Repeating this process, we get a trivialization of
\mathcal{L}
. It follows that the restriction map (
5.6
) is injective. Hence, the central charge map (
5.7
) is injective.

We now prove the surjectivity. For each
p_{i}\in R
, there exists a
\Gamma
-stable open neighborhood
U_{i}
of
p
with a
\Gamma
-equivariant étale map
U_{i}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
, where
\mathbb{A}^{1}
carries the standard
\Gamma
-action as in (
4.37
). Observe that
p_{i}
is the only ramified point in
U_{i}
. By Corollary
4.8
(1), there is a natural morphism
\pi_{i}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{U}_{i}},U_{i}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{U_{i}}
be the pull-back of the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
via the map
\pi_{i}
. By the injectivity of (
5.7
), the central charge map
c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},U_{i}})\to\mathbb{Z}
is injective. Since
c(\mathcal{L}_{U_{i}})=1
, we must have
c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},U_{i}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}
. Note that
\{\mathring{C},U_{1},\ldots,U_{r}\}
forms an open covering of
C
. Moreover, by Lemma
5.2
there are unique isomorphisms between the restrictions of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{C}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{U_{i}}
on their intersections, since their restrictions are the level one generator in
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},U_{i}\setminus\{p_{i}\}})
or
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},U_{i}\cap U_{j}})
for any
i,j
. Therefore, they can be glued to be a line bundle of central charge one in
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})
, which will be denoted by
\mathcal{L}_{C}
. It follows that the central charge map
c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\to\mathbb{Z}
is an isomorphism.
∎

In the proof of the injectivity of the map (
5.6
), we used a similar argument as in
[
39
]
[Lemma 4.3.3]. In fact, using the same argument we have the following more general result, which will be used later.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let C be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action and assume that all ramified points in
C
are totally ramified. Let
Z
be a closed subvariety in
C^{n}
. Let
D
be an irreducible closed subvariety of codimension one in
C^{n}\backslash Z
. Then, the following restriction map is an embedding

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}\setminus Z})\to\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}\setminus(Z\cup D)}).\qed

We define the action of the symmetric group
S_{n}
and
\Gamma^{n}
on
C^{n}
by
s\cdot(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}):=(p_{s(1)},\ldots,p_{s(n)})
and
(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n})\cdot(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}):=(\gamma_{1}p_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n}p_{n})
, where
s\in S_{n}
,
\gamma_{i}\in\Gamma
. Let
Z
be a closed subvariety in
C^{n}
that is stable under the actions of
S_{n}
and
\Gamma^{n}
. Recall that for the finest partition
\xi=(\{1\},\ldots,\{n\})
of
[n]
, there is a factorization

(5.8)

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z}\simeq(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})|_{C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z}.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})
. We define a map

(5.9)

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\to\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z})

by
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n})\mapsto(\mathcal{L}_{1}\boxtimes\ldots\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{n})|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z}}
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let C be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action and assume that all ramified points in
C
are totally ramified. Let
n\geq 2
and
Z
be a closed subvariety in
C^{n}
which is stable under the actions of
S_{n}
and
\Gamma^{n}
. We further assume that the diagonal
\Delta_{C^{n}}
is contained in
C^{n}\setminus Z
. Let
\xi
be the finest partition of
[n]
. Then,

(1)

The map (
5.9
) is an isomorphism, whose inverse induces the following isomorphism

(5.10)

c_{\xi}:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{\xi}^{n}\setminus Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{n}

given by
\mathcal{L}\mapsto(c_{1},\ldots,c_{n})
, where
c_{i}=c\big(\mathcal{L}|_{(e_{1},\ldots,e_{i-1})\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},q_{i}}\times(e_{i+1},\ldots,e_{n})}\big)
and
e_{j}
is the base point in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},q_{j}}
for any
(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n})\in C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z
. We will call
c_{\xi}
the multi-central charge map.

(2)

The restriction map
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}\setminus Z})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{\xi}^{n}\setminus Z})
is an embedding.
Moreover, for any
\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}\setminus Z})
, if
c(\mathcal{L}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\Delta_{C^{n}}}})=a
, then
c_{\xi}(\mathcal{L}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{\xi}^{n}\setminus Z}})=(a,a,\ldots,a)
. As a consequence, the central charge map (
5.3
) is injective.

Proof.

When n=2,
\xi=(\{1\},\{2\})
is the finest partition. We fix an arbitrary point
(q_{1},q_{2})\in C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z
and denote
C_{q_{1}}:=\{p\in C\mid(q_{1},p)\in C^{2}_{\xi}\setminus Z\}
,
C_{q_{2}}:=\{p\in C\mid(p,q_{2})\in C^{2}_{\xi}\setminus Z\}
. Clearly,
C_{q_{i}}
is open in
C
. If
C_{q_{1}}
is empty, then
(q_{1},p)\not\in C^{2}_{\xi}\setminus Z
for all
p\in C
. It follows that
(q_{1},p)\in Z
for all
p\in C\setminus\Gamma\cdot\{p\}
. Since
Z
is closed, we conclude that
\{q_{1}\}\times C\subseteq Z
. Hence,
(q_{1},q_{2})\in Z
, which is a contradiction. Therefore,
C_{q_{1}}
is non-empty. Similarly,
C_{q_{2}}
is also non-empty. Let
\pi_{1}:C^{2}\setminus Z\to C
be the map given by
(p_{1},p_{2})\mapsto p_{1}
. Then
\pi_{1}
must be surjective. Otherwise, there is a point
p\in C
such that
\pi_{1}^{-1}(p)=\big(\{p\}\times C\big)\setminus Z
is empty. Then,
p\times C\subseteq Z
contradicts with
\Delta_{C^{2}}\subseteq Z
.

Let
e_{q_{i}}\in\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},q_{i}}
be the base point. Consider the following map

(5.11)

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}((\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})|_{C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z})\to\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{2}}})\times\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{1}}})

given by
\mathcal{L}\mapsto(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})
, where
\mathcal{L}_{1}:=\mathcal{L}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{2}}}\times e_{q_{2}}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}:=\mathcal{L}|_{e_{q_{1}}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{1}}}}
are regarded as line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{i}}}
respectively. We claim (
5.11
) is an isomorphism.

We first prove the injectivity. By Theorem
5.3
, we have isomorphisms

(5.12)

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\simeq\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{i}}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

For any
\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}((\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})|_{C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z}))
, by the isomorphism (
5.12
),
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
uniquely extend to rigidified line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}
, which will be still denoted by
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
. Set

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{L}^{-1}\otimes(\mathcal{L}_{1}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{2})|_{C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z}.

By Lemma
5.1
,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the pullback of some line bundle
M
on
C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z
via the projection
\pi:(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})|_{C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z}\to C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z
. Note that
M\simeq e^{*}\pi^{*}M\simeq e^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is trivial. Then,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\simeq\pi^{*}M
is trivial. Thus,
\mathcal{L}\simeq(\mathcal{L}_{1}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{2})|_{C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z}
. This proves the injectivity of (
5.11
).

Given any
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{2}}})\times\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{1}}})
, we still denote their extensions to
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}
by
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})
. Clearly, the line bundle
(\mathcal{L}_{1}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{2})|_{C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}((\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})|_{C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z})
is mapping to
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})
via the map (
5.11
). This shows that (
5.11
) is also surjective.

In summary, we have an isomorphism

(5.13)

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z})\simeq\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C})\times\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}).

From construction, this map is exactly the inverse map of (
5.9
), and it gives rise to the multi-central charge map (
5.10
). This completes the proof of part (1) for
n=2
.

By Proposition
5.4
, the following composition map is injective,

\iota:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{2}\setminus Z})\to\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{\xi}^{2}\setminus Z})\xrightarrow{c_{\xi}}\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}.

Given
\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{2}\setminus Z})
such that
\iota(\mathcal{L})=(a,b)
, i.e.
c(\mathcal{L}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{2}}}\times e_{q_{2}}})=a
and
c(\mathcal{L}|_{e_{q_{1}}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{q_{1}}}})=b
for any
(q_{1},q_{2})\in C^{2}\backslash Z
. Suppose that
the central charge of
\mathcal{L}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},q_{1})}}
is
d
. We follow an argument in
[
39
, Lemma 3.4.3]
to show
b=d=a
.
Consider the following convolution and projection morphisms

\mathfrak{m}:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{2}\setminus Z}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{2}\setminus Z},\quad\mathrm{pr}:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},C^{2}\setminus Z}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C}.

Note that
\mathrm{pr}^{-1}(e_{q_{1}})\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{1}}
, where
C_{1}:=C_{q_{1}}\cup\Gamma\cdot q_{1}
. We regard
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:=(\mathfrak{m}^{*}\mathcal{L})|_{\mathrm{pr}^{-1}(e_{q})}
as a line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{1}}
. We denote by
\phi
the composition map
\mathrm{pr}^{-1}(e_{q_{1}})\cap\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},q_{1})}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},q_{1}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},q_{1})}
. Then,
\phi
is exactly the restriction of the local convolution map
\mathfrak{m}_{(q_{1},q_{1})}:\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},q_{1})}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},q_{1})}
. Thus, the central charge of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}|_{q_{1}}\simeq\phi^{*}(\mathcal{L}|_{(q_{1},q_{1})})
is equal to the central charge of
\mathcal{L}_{(q_{1},q_{1})}
which is
d
.

On the other hand, for any
p\in C_{q_{1}}
, we have an isomorphism
\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},p)}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}_{(q_{1},p)}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},p)}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},q_{1}}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},p}
. This isomorphism restricts to an isomorphism
\psi:\mathrm{pr}^{-1}(e_{q_{1}})\cap\mathrm{Conv}_{\mathcal{G},(q_{1},p)}\simeq e_{q_{1}}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},p}
. Thus, the central charge of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}|_{p}\simeq\psi^{*}(\mathcal{L}|_{e_{q_{1}}\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},p}})
is
b
. Since
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is a line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{1}}
, the central charge of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}|_{x}
is constant along
x\in C_{1}
. It follows that
b=d
. Since,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{2}\setminus Z}
is
S_{2}
-symmetric, we also have
a=d
. This completes the proof for
n=2
.

When
n\geq 3
, let
\xi
be the finest partition of
[n]
. Fix an arbitrary point
(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n})\in C_{\xi}\setminus Z
. Denote by
e_{i}
the base point in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},q_{i}}
. By induction on
n
and using the same argument as in proving the isomorphism (
5.13
), one can prove that (
5.9
) is an isomorphism and its inverse map gives rise to the multi-central charge map (
5.10
).
Moreover, there is a commutative diagram

(5.14)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 98.7723pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.06696pt\raise Z_{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern
