where
\xi^{\prime}
is the minimal partition of
[n-1]
,
x=(e_{1},\ldots,e_{n-1})
,
y=(e_{2},\ldots,e_{n})
,
Z_{x}=\{\,p\in C\,|\,(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n-1},p)\not\in C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z\,\}
,
Z_{y}=\{\,p\in C\,|\,(p,q_{2},\ldots,q_{n})\not\in C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z\,\}
,
Z_{n}=\{\vec{p}\in C^{n-1}\,|\,(\vec{p},q_{n})\not\in C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z\}
, and
Z_{1}=\{\vec{p}\in C^{n-1}\,|\,(q_{1},\vec{p})\not\in C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z\}
.
By Proposition
5.4
, the following composition map is injective,

\iota:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}\setminus Z})\to\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z})\xrightarrow{c_{\xi}}\mathbb{Z}^{n}.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{2}\setminus Z})
. Suppose
\iota(\mathcal{L})=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
. By induction on
n
and the commutativity of (
5.14
), we have
a_{1}=a_{2}=\cdots=a_{n}=c(\mathcal{L})
.
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
X
be an irreducible smooth
\Gamma
-curve consisting of exactly one ramified point
p
. Suppose
f:X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant étale morphism sending
p
to
0
, where the
\Gamma
-action on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is defined by (
4.37
). Let
n\geq 2
and
Z=\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in X^{n}\,|\,\bar{x}_{i}\neq\bar{x}_{j},\overline{f(x_{i})}=\overline{f(x_{j})}\text{ for some }i,j\}
. There is an isomorphism

c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

Proof.

We first show that
Z
is a closed subvariety in
X^{n}
. When
n=2
,
Z=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in X^{2}\,|\,\bar{x}_{1}\neq\bar{x}_{2},\overline{f(x_{1})}=\overline{f(x_{2})}\}
. Note that
Z=(X\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X)\backslash\Delta_{X}
. Since
f
is étale, the diagonal morphism
\Delta:X\to X\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}X
is an open embedding, cf.
[
35
,
Tag 06CR
]
and
[
35
,
Tag 05W1
]
. Thus,
Z
is a closed subvariety of
X\times X
.
When
n\geq 3
, let
Z_{i,j}=\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in X^{n}\,|\,\bar{x}_{i}\neq\bar{x}_{j},\overline{f(x_{i})}=\overline{f(x_{j})}\}
. Then,
Z_{i,j}\simeq X^{n-2}\times Z^{\prime}
is closed in
X^{n}
, where
Z^{\prime}:=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in X^{2}\,|\,\bar{x}_{1}\neq\bar{x}_{2},\overline{f(x_{1})}=\overline{f(x_{2})}\}
. Thus,
Z=\bigcup_{i,j}Z_{i,j}
is closed in
X^{n}
.

Given any
x\in X
, in view of Proposition
5.5
, the map
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{X^{n}\setminus Z})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}
sending
\mathcal{L}
to the central charge of
\mathcal{L}|_{(x,\ldots,x)}
is an embedding.
On the other hand, by part (1) of Corollary
4.8
, we have an isomorphism

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}(X^{n}\setminus Z).

Consider the natural morphism
\phi:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Pulling back
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
via
\phi
, we get a line bundle
\phi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z}
. Note that
(\phi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})|_{(x,\ldots,x)}
has the same central charge as
(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})|_{(f(x),\ldots,f(x))}
, which is
1
. It follows that
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{X}},X^{n}\setminus Z})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}
is also surjective.
∎

Proposition 5.7
.

(1)

Let
X
be an irreducible smooth curve. Let
G
be a simply-connected simple algebraic group. Then, the central charge map
c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{G,X^{n}})\to\mathbb{Z}
is an isomorphism.

(2)

Let
X
be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action and assume that all points in
X
are unramified. Then, the central charge map
c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}})\to\mathbb{Z}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from
[
39
, Corollary 3.4.4.]
. It can also be shown by applying Proposition
5.5
to the case when
\Gamma=\{e\}
.

For part (2), by Corollary
4.8
(2), we have an isomorphism
\alpha:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus Z}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{G,X^{n}\setminus Z}
for
Z=\{\vec{p}\in X^{n}\mid p_{i}=\gamma\cdot p_{j}\text{ for some }1\leq i,j\leq n,\gamma\neq e\}
. By part (1), the central charge map
c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{G,X^{n}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{G,X^{n}\setminus Z})\to\mathbb{Z}
is an isomorphism. Let
L_{X^{n}\setminus Z}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{G,X^{n}\setminus Z})
denote the restriction of the level one line bundle
L_{X^{n}}
constructed in part (1). Pulling back
L_{X^{n}\setminus Z}
along
\alpha
, we get a rigidified line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus Z}
, denoted by
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus Z}
. Let
\vec{\gamma}\in\Gamma^{n}
. Consider the isomorphism
\vec{\gamma}:X^{n}\to X^{n}
given by
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\mapsto(\gamma_{1}p_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n}p_{n})
. It induces an isomorphism
\vec{\gamma}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus Z}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)}
. Pushing forward
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus Z}
along
\vec{\gamma}
, we get a rigidified line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)})
.

Note that
\vec{\gamma}(Z)=\{\vec{p}\in X^{n}\mid\text{there exist }1\leq i,j\leq n\text{ such that }p_{i}=\gamma\cdot p_{j}\text{ for some }\gamma\neq\gamma_{i}\gamma_{j}^{-1}\}
. We have
\bigcap_{\vec{\gamma}}\vec{\gamma}(Z)=\emptyset
. Hence,
\{X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)\}_{\vec{\gamma}}
is a open cover of
X^{n}
. By Propostition
5.4
, the restiction map
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus(\vec{\gamma}(Z)\cup\vec{\gamma}^{\prime}(Z))})\to\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}_{\xi}})
is injective, where
\xi
is the finest partition of
[n]
. By Proposition
5.5
, this induces an embedding

(5.15)

\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus(\vec{\gamma}(Z)\cup\vec{\gamma}^{\prime}(Z))})\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{n}

for any
\vec{\gamma},\vec{\gamma}^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{n}
. From construction, the images of the restrictions of line bundles
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)}
and
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)}
to
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus(\vec{\gamma}(Z)\cup\vec{\gamma}^{\prime}(Z))}
under the map (
5.15
) are
(1,1,\ldots,1)
. By Lemma
5.2
, we conclude that the restrictions of
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)}
and
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}^{\prime}(Z)}
to the intersection
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}\setminus(\vec{\gamma}(Z)\cup\vec{\gamma}^{\prime}(Z))}
are isomorphic. Hence, we can glue these line bundles
\{\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}\setminus\vec{\gamma}(Z)}\}_{\vec{\gamma}}
. This produces a line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},X^{n}}
, denoted by
\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}}
. From construction,
c(\mathcal{L}_{X^{n}})=1
. Therefore,
c
is an isomorphism. ∎

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
C
be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action and assume that all ramified points in
C
are totally ramified.

(1)

For
n\geq 1
, the central charge map (
5.3
) is an isomorphism

c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

(2)

For
n\geq 2
, let
\xi=(I,J)
be a partition of
[n]
. Let
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{L}_{I}
,
\mathcal{L}_{J}
be rigidified line bundles over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I}}
,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{J}}
respectively. Suppose that they have the same central charge. Then, we have an isomorphism of line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}

\mathcal{L}|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq(\mathcal{L}_{I}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{J})|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}.

Proof.

The case when
n=1
has been proved in Theorem
5.3
.

For
n\geq 2
, the central charge map
c
is injective by Proposition
5.5
. Let
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
be a nontrivial partition. Since
|I_{j}|<n
, by induction on
n
, we have an isomorphism
c:\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{j}}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{j}}}
be the rigidified line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{j}}}
such that
c(\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{j}}})=1
. Recall that we have a factorization
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z}\simeq(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{1}}}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{I_{k}}})|_{C^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z}
. Set
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}:=(\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{1}}}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{k}}})|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}}
.

Let
R:=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\}
be the set of ramified points in
C
. Set
\mathring{C}=C\backslash R
. By Proposition
5.7
,
\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(\mathring{C})^{n}})\simeq\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{C}^{n}}
denote the level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(\mathring{C})^{n}}
constructed in Proposition
5.7
. On the other hand, for each
p_{i}
, there is a neighborhood
U_{i}
of
p_{i}
with a
\Gamma
-equivariant étale morphism
f_{i}:U_{i}\to\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Let

(5.16)

Z_{i}:=\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in U_{i}^{n}\,|\,\bar{x}_{a}\neq\bar{x}_{b},\overline{f_{i}(x_{a})}=\overline{f_{i}(x_{b})}\text{ for some }1\leq a<b\leq n\}

be a closed subvariety in
U_{i}^{n}
. By Lemma
5.6
, pulling back
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
via the natural map
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{U}_{i}},(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, we get a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{e}(\mathrm{Gr}_{(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}})
whose central charge is
1
. Note that
\big\{C^{n}_{\xi},(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i},(\mathring{C})^{n}\big\}_{\xi,i}
is an open covering of
C^{n}
, and pairwise intersections are of the form
X^{n}_{\xi}
,
X^{n}_{\xi}\setminus Z_{X}
, or
X^{n}\setminus Z_{X}
for some
\Gamma
-stable open subset
X
in
C
and some closed subset
Z_{X}
in
X^{n}
. In view of Proposition
5.5
and Lemma
5.6
, the restrictions of rigidified line bundles
\{\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}_{(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{C}^{n}}\}_{\xi,i,}
are isomorphic on the pairwise intersections. Thus, we can glue these line bundles and get a factorizable rigidified line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
whose central charge is
1
. Hence,
c
is also surjective. Part (2) follows from the fact that
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}}\simeq(\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{1}}}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{k}}})|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}_{\xi}}}
.
∎

We remark that the proof of Theorem
5.8
also works when
\Gamma
is a trivial group. We call the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
constructed in Theorem
5.8
the level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
.

5.2.
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}

In this subsection, we will show that when
G
is simply-connected, there is a unique
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure on the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
, where
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
is the jet group scheme defined in Definition
4.3
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
(here we think of line bundles from the point of view as in Appendix
C
). An
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}
is a collection of isomorphisms
\{\phi_{g,x}:\mathcal{L}_{x}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{gx}\mid g\in L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}(S),x\in\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C}^{n}}(S)\}
for any
\mathbb{C}
-scheme
S
, satisfying the cocycle conditions
\phi_{g^{\prime},gx}\circ\phi_{g,x}=\phi_{g^{\prime}g,x}
. We define a group scheme

(5.17)

\widehat{L^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{C^{n}}(S):=\left\{\big(g,\{\phi_{g,x}\}\big)\,\middle|\,g\in L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}},\phi_{g,x}:\mathcal{L}_{x}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{gx},x\in\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C}^{n}}(S)\right\},

where the multiplication is defined by
(g^{\prime},\{\phi_{g^{\prime},x}\})\cdot(g,\{\psi_{g,x}\})=(g^{\prime}g,\{\phi_{g^{\prime},gx}\circ\psi_{g,x}\})
. One can easily check the associativity. By definition,
\mathcal{L}
has an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure if and only if the following central extension has a splitting
\beta:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\widehat{L^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{C^{n}}

(5.18)

K\to\widehat{L^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{C^{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}}L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}},

where
K=\{\phi\mid\phi:\mathcal{L}\simeq\mathcal{L}\}
is a group scheme over
C^{n}
, and
\mathrm{pr}:\widehat{L^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{C^{n}}\to L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
is the projection map. Conversely, if the extension (
5.18
) has a splitting
\beta:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\widehat{L^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{C^{n}}
, say
\beta(g)=(g,\{\phi_{g,x}\})
, then it gives a collection of isomorphisms
\{\phi_{g,x}:\mathcal{L}_{x}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{gx}\mid g\in L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}(S),x\in\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C}^{n}}(S)\}
. Since
\beta
is a group homomorphism, we have
(g^{\prime},\{\phi_{g^{\prime},x}\})\cdot(g,\{\phi_{g,x}\})=(g^{\prime}g,\{\phi_{g^{\prime}g,x}\})
. Hence
\phi_{g^{\prime},gx}\circ\phi_{g,x}=\phi_{g^{\prime}g,x}
, which implies that
\mathcal{L}
is
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant. Therefore, the extension (
5.18
) has a splitting if and only if
\mathcal{L}
is
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant.

Lemma 5.9
.

If the extension (
5.18
) has a splitting
\beta:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to\widehat{L^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{C^{n}}
, then it is unique.

Proof.

Let
\beta_{1}
,
\beta_{2}
be two splittings, which give a group homomorphism
\phi:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}\to K
sending
g\in L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
to
\beta_{1}(g)\cdot\beta_{2}(g)^{-1}
. For any
\vec{p}\in C^{n}
, we have an isomorphism
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\vec{p}}\simeq\prod_{j=1}^{k}L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{q_{j}}
, where
\bigsqcup_{j}\Gamma\cdot q_{j}=\bigcup_{i}\Gamma\cdot p_{i}
. Over the fiber at
\vec{p}
, the morphism
\phi_{\vec{p}}:L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\vec{p}}\to K_{\vec{p}}
is trivial since the character of each
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{q_{j}}
is trivial, (when
q_{j}
is unramified, the character of
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{q_{j}}\simeq G(\mathcal{O})
is trivial, cf.
[
32
, Corollary 9.1.3]
; when
q_{j}
is ramified, there is a quotient map
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{q_{j}}\to G^{\sigma}
whose kernel is pro-unipotent, hence the character of
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{q_{j}}
is trivial since
G^{\sigma}
is simple, cf.
[
4
, Table 2.3]
.) Thus
\phi
is trivial.
∎

From now on, we take
C
to be
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with the standard
\Gamma
-action as in (
4.37
).

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Given an integer
l>0
, if the
l
-th power
\mathcal{L}^{l}
of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}
has an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure, then so does
\mathcal{L}
.

Before proving this proposition, we first make some preparations. By Proposition
4.25
, to show a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
has an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure, it suffices to consider the restriction of
\mathcal{L}
to each BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.

For any integer
k
, we define a group scheme
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{n}}
as follows, which is of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
, for each
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
R
,

(5.19)

L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{n}}(R):=G\bigg(\frac{R[t,z_{1},\ldots,z_{n}]}{\big(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma}(t-\gamma\cdot z_{i})^{k}\big)}\bigg)^{\Gamma}.

Set
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}=L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{n}}\times_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{n}}\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Clearly,
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}=\varprojlim L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. When
\xi
is the finest partition of
[n]
, we have a factorization

L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}\simeq\big(L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times\cdots\times L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\big)|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}.

Lemma 5.11
.

The action of
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
on
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
factors through the action of
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
for sufficiently large integer
k
.

Proof.

Let
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}=\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}
. When
n=1
, we have
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{G}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
and
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq G_{\mathcal{O}}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, cf.
[
39
, Lemma 3.4.2]
. For any integer
k
, we have
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq G_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, where
\mathcal{O}_{k}:=\mathbb{C}[t]/(t^{k})
. It is well-known that the
G_{\mathcal{O}}
-action on
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda}
factors through
G_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}
for some
k>0
. Thus, the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
-action on the Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
factors through the action of
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
for this
k
.
Denote by
K_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
the kernel of the morphism
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\to L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}
. Then it suffices to show
K_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
acts trivially on the Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
. This is equivalent to that the composition of morphisms
K_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\simeq K_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\xrightarrow{s^{\lambda}}K_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{act}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}
agrees with the constant morphism
K_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\to{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\xrightarrow{s^{\lambda}}\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}
. This is true over
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. Hence they must agree over
{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
, since
K_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is irreducible.

When
n\geq 2
, let
\xi=(\{1\},\ldots,\{n\})
be the finest partition of
[n]
. We have

\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq\Big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times\dots\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{n}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}\Big)\big|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}.

Thus, the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}
-action on the Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}}_{\xi}^{n}}
factors through the action of
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}
for some
k>0
. By the same argument as before, for this
k
, the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-action on the Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}}^{n}}
factors through the action of
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
X
and
Y
be smooth schemes over
S
, where
X,Y
and
S
are all schemes of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
. Suppose that
f:X\to Y
is a morphism over
S
such that
f_{s}:X_{s}\to Y_{s}
is an isomorphism on the fiber for each
s\in S
, then
f:X\to Y
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is taken from
[
13
]
. By assumption
X
and
Y
are flat over
S
and
f_{s}
is an isomorphism for each
s\in S
, then
f
is a bijection, unramified, flat morphism. In particular,
f
is étale and injective on the
\mathbb{C}
points of
X
and
Y
. It follows that
f
is an open immersion and also a bijection. Hence
f
is an isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
\phi:G\to H
be a morphism of connected affine algebraic groups over
\mathbb{C}
, such that the induced morphism
d\phi:\mathrm{Lie}\,(G)\to\mathrm{Lie}\,(H)
of their Lie algebras is an isomorphism. If
H
is simply-connected, then
\phi
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

cf.
[
33
, Exercise 5.3.5]

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.10
.

It suffices to prove this proposition for each variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. By Lemma
5.11
, the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-action factors through the action of the group scheme
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
for some
k
(depending on
\vec{\lambda}
). We still denote by
\mathcal{L}
its restriction to
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.

By assumption,
\mathcal{L}^{l}
has an
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure on
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, i.e. there are isomorphisms
\Phi_{g,x}:\mathcal{L}^{l}_{x}\simeq\mathcal{L}^{l}_{gx}
such that
\Phi_{g^{\prime},gx}\circ\Phi_{g,x}=\Phi_{g^{\prime}g,x}
for any
g,g^{\prime}\in L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
,
x\in\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.
We define a groups scheme
\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
as follows, for each
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
R
,

(5.20)

\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}(R):=\left\{\big(g,\{\phi_{g,x}\}\big)\,\middle|\,\begin{aligned} &g\in L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}(R),\phi_{g,x}:\mathcal{L}_{x}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{gx}\text{ such that }\\
&\phi_{g,x}^{l}=\Phi_{g,x}\text{ for }x\in\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}(R)\end{aligned}\right\}.

There is a central extension

(5.21)

1_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to Z_{l}\to\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\xrightarrow{p}{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to 1_{\mathbb{A}^{n}},

where
Z_{l}=\{\phi:\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}\mid\phi^{l}=\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{L}^{l}\to\mathcal{L}^{l}\}
is a finite group scheme over
\mathbb{A}^{n}
. To prove our proposition, it suffices to show that this central extension splits.

When
n=1
, set
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}=\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}
. There is a splitting over
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

since
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{G}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
and
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq G_{\mathcal{O}}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.
Let
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\dagger}
be the schematic image of
\beta:L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\to\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
(we remove the non-identity components over
0
if they occur). By
[
21
, Proposition 9.8]
,
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is a flat group scheme over
{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. By Cartier’s Theorem and
[
21
, Theorem 10.2]
,
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is smooth over
{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. We claim that the natural morphism
p:L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\rightarrow L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is an isomorphism. By Lemma
5.12
, it suffices to check the isomorphism
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{x}\simeq L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{x}
of fibers over any
x\in{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. It is true over
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, since there is splitting
\beta
over
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. When
x=0
, we have

(5.22)

1\to\mu_{l}\to\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{0}\to{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{0}\to 1,

where
\mu_{\ell}
denote the group of
\ell
-th root of unity. Since
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
and
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
are both smooth group schemes over
{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
and isomorphic over
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, their fibers at
0
have the same dimension. By (
5.22
),
\mathrm{Lie}(\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{0})\simeq\mathrm{Lie}({L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{0})
. By dimension consideration, the injective map

\mathrm{Lie}(L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{0})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Lie}(\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{0})\simeq\mathrm{Lie}(L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{0})

must be an isomorphism. Moreover, the kernel of the quotient map
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{0}\to G^{\sigma}
is unipotent. By
[
24
, Lemma 6.1]
,
G^{\sigma}
is simply-connected. Thus,
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{0}
is also simply-connected. In view of Lemma
5.13
, the morphism
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{0}\to L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{0}
is an isomorphism. Finally, by Lemma
5.12
, the morphism
p:L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\dagger}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\to L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is also an isomorphism. This gives a splitting of the central extension (
5.21
).

When
n=2
, there exists an isomorphism

(5.23)

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times\{1\}}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

given by
(a,b,\mathcal{F},\beta)\mapsto\big((b^{-1}a,1,(\bar{\rho}_{b^{-1}})_{*}\mathcal{F},(\bar{\rho}_{b^{-1}})_{*}\beta),b\big)
, where
\bar{\rho}_{b^{-1}}:\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
is given by
\bar{c}\mapsto\overline{b^{-1}c}
. This isomorphism is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, where on the left the action is induced from the simultaneous dilation on
{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
and on the right the action is induced from the dilation on
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.

Let
U={\mathbb{A}^{1}}\setminus\{1,\sigma(1),\ldots,\sigma^{m-1}(1)\}
. By the factorization property, we have
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}_{\mathcal{G},U\times\{1\}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{\mathcal{G},U}\times\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{G}^{\lambda_{2}}
. Thus, we have a splitting over
U\times\{1\}
,

(5.24)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z_{l}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.5871pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 79.5871pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{U\times\{1\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.53323pt\raise-5.18748pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.52148pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 141.52148pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 128.32722pt\raise 15.95833pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 4.02126pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 219.74756pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 219.74756pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

By the same argument as in the case when
n=1
, we can extend the splitting
\beta
to a splitting
\alpha
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\{1\}
. Via the isomorphism (
5.23
), the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-dilation produces a splitting
\alpha_{1}:L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\to\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
from
\alpha
. By a similar argument, there exists a splitting
\alpha_{2}:L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}\to\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. By the uniqueness of splitting,
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
agree on
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
. Thus, we get a splitting over
(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1})\cup(\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
. By Hartogs’ Lemma, this splitting extends to
\mathbb{A}^{1}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

When
n>2
, we prove it by induction on
n
. For any nontrivial partition
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{j})
of
[n]
, we have factorizations
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}\simeq(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{I_{1}}}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{I_{j}}})|_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}
and
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}\simeq(L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{1}}}\times\cdots\times L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{j}}})|_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}
. By induction, there is a splitting

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z_{l}|_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.19571pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 84.19571pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 117.51428pt\raise-5.18748pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 130.50253pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 130.50253pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.39159pt\raise 16.29443pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\beta_{\xi}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 103.60483pt\raise 4.02126pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 193.10104pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 193.10104pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Moreover,
\beta_{\xi}
and
\beta_{\xi^{\prime}}
agree on
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime}}
by the uniqueness of splitting. Hence, we can glue these morphisms
\{\beta_{\xi}\}_{\xi}
and obtain a splitting
\beta:L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\bigcup\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}\to\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\bigcup\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}
over
\bigcup_{\xi}\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}
. Note that the complement of
\bigcup_{\xi}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}
is
\Delta_{[n]}
, which has codimension
\geq 2
. By Hartogs’ Lemma,
\beta
extends to a unique morphism
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\overline{L^{+}\mathcal{G}}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.
∎

Remark 5.14
.

When
\Gamma
is taken to be the trivial group, we have
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}=\mathrm{Gr}_{G,C^{n}}
. Clearly the proofs of Lemma
5.9
and Proposition
5.10
are still valid.

Following
[
14
]
, we can construct a determinant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C}^{n}}
, which has a natural
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-action. The adjoint representation
\mathrm{Ad}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal{V}_{0})
induces a morphism
\phi:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal{V}_{0}),C^{n}}
. For each morphism
\mathrm{Spec}(R)\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal{V}_{0}),C^{n}}
, i.e. a triple
\big(\vec{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}),\mathcal{V},\beta:\mathcal{V}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\cup\Gamma_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\simeq\mathcal{V}|_{\bar{C}_{R}\setminus\cup\Gamma_{\bar{p}_{i}}}\big)
, where
p_{i}\in C_{R}
, and
\mathcal{V}
is a vector bundle on
\bar{C}_{R}
, there exists
N>0
such that

\mathcal{V}_{0}(-N(\sum\Gamma_{\bar{p}_{i}}))\subseteq\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{V}_{0}(N(\sum\Gamma_{\bar{p}_{i}}))
. We associate a line bundle

(5.25)

\textstyle\bigwedge^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{V}_{0}(N(\sum\Gamma_{\bar{p}_{i}})/\mathcal{V})\otimes\bigwedge^{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{V}_{0}(N(\sum\Gamma_{\bar{p}_{i}})/\mathcal{V}_{0})^{-1}

on
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
. This assignment gives a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal{V}_{0}),C^{n}}
. Then the pullback
\phi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
is a line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C^{n}}
with a natural
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-action, which will be still denoted by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
.

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
\Gamma
be either trivial or generated by
\sigma
. Let
C
be an irreducible smooth curve with a faithful
\Gamma
-action such that all ramified points in
C
are totally ramified. Then, there is an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure on the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},{C}^{n}}
.

Proof.

We first prove the case when
\Gamma
is trivial and
\mathcal{G}
is the constant group scheme. Let
\{U_{i}\}
be a cover of
C
with étale morphisms
U_{i}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
. By Proposition
4.4
, we have the following isomorphism for some
Z_{i}
closed in
U_{i}^{n}
,

(5.26)

\mathrm{Gr}_{G,U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathbb{A}^{n}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}(U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}).

Note that the determinant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathbb{A}^{n}}
automatically has a trivialization along the section
e
. By Theorem
5.7
(1), we have
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}\simeq L_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{l}
for some
l>0
, where
L_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
denotes the level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Applying Proposition
5.10
to the case when
\Gamma
is trivial, we get an
L^{+}G_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure on
L_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Since the isomorphism (
5.26
) commutes with the
L^{+}G_{U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}
action, the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}:=\phi^{*}(L_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}
is equipped with an
L^{+}G_{U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}
-equivariant structure, where
\phi:\mathrm{Gr}_{G,U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathbb{A}^{n}}
is the natural morphism induced from (
5.26
). When
n=1
,
Z_{i}
is empty, the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,C}
can be recovered by gluing these rigidified line bundles
\mathcal{L}_{U_{i}}
. Hence,
\mathcal{L}_{C}
also has an
L^{+}G_{C}
-equivariant structure by the uniqueness of splitting, cf. Lemma
5.9
. When
n\geq 2
, for any non-trivial partition
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
of
[n]
, the restriction of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq(\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{1}}}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{k}}})|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
has an
{L}^{+}G_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
-equivariant structure by induction. Note that
\{U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i},C^{n}_{\xi}\}_{i,\xi}
is a cover of
C^{n}
and the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
can be recovered by gluing the rigidified line bundles
\{\mathcal{L}_{(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}},\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}\}_{i,\xi}
. Again, by the uniqueness of splitting,
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
admits an
L^{+}G_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure.

When
\Gamma
is the cyclic group generated by the standard automorphism
\sigma
, let
\{\mathring{C},U_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r}
be the covering of
C
constructed in Theorem
5.8
. Recall Proposition
5.7
for the construction of the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{(\mathring{C})^{n}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},(\mathring{C})^{n}}
. By the case when
\Gamma
is trivial and the uniqueness of splitting,
\mathcal{L}_{(\mathring{C})^{n}}
has an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{(\mathring{C})^{n}}
-equivariant structure. For each
i
, by Corollary
4.8
, we have a base change map

(5.27)

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}(U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}),

where
Z_{i}
is define in (
5.16
). By the same argument as in the case when
\Gamma
is trivial, the level one line bundle

\mathcal{L}_{(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},U_{i}^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}
has an
{L}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}}
-equivariant structure for
1\leq i\leq r
. We prove the theorem by induction on
n
. When
n=1
, the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C}
is constructed by gluing the rigidified line bundles
\{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{C}},\mathcal{L}_{U_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{r}
. Thus,
\mathcal{L}_{C}
is equipped with an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C}
-equivariant structure naturally by the uniqueness of splitting, cf. Lemma
5.9
. When
n\geq 2
, for any non-trivial partition
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
of
[n]
, the restriction of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq(\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{1}}}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{C^{I_{k}}})|_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
has an
{L}^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}
-equivariant structure by induction. As in the proof of Theorem
5.8
, the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
can constructed by gluing the rigidified line bundles
\{\mathcal{L}_{(\mathring{C})^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{(U_{i})^{n}\setminus Z_{i}},\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}_{\xi}}\}_{i,\xi}
. Again, by the uniqueness of splitting,
\mathcal{L}_{C^{n}}
is equipped with an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{C^{n}}
-equivariant structure.
∎

5.3.
Level one line bundle on BD Grassmannians of adjoint type

In this subsection, we assume that
G
is of adjoint type. Let
G^{\prime}
be the simply-connected cover of
G
. Let
T^{\prime}
be the maximal torus of
G^{\prime}
which is the preimage of
T
via the projection
G^{\prime}\to G
. We have the natural embedding
X_{*}(T^{\prime})\subseteq X_{*}(T)
.

Recall that the components of affine Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{G}
can be parameterized by the set
X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}
. Let
\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the affine line with a coordinate
z
and the standard
\Gamma
-action as in (
4.37
). Set
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}=\mathbb{A}^{1}-0
. Then,
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{G}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, cf.
[
39
, Lemma 3.4.2]
. Thus, the components of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
can be parameterized by the elements of
X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}
. For any
\kappa\in X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}
, denote by
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}[\kappa]
the component corresponding to
\kappa
. Let
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}[\kappa]
denote the closure of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}[\kappa]
in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}
, and we call it the
\kappa
-component of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}
.

More generally, for
n\geq 2
, let
\xi
be the finest partition of
[n]
, then there is a factorization over the open subset
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{\xi}:=\{\vec{p}\in(\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1})^{n}\mid\bar{p}_{i}\neq\bar{p}_{j}\ \forall i\neq j\}
of
\mathbb{A}^{n}

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq(\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})|_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{\xi}}.

For any
\vec{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n})\in\big(X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}\big)^{n}
, let
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}_{\xi}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]:=(\prod\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}[\kappa_{i}])|_{\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{n}_{\xi}}
. Define
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
to be the closure of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}_{\xi}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
in
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, and again call it the
\vec{\kappa}
-component of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. When
\vec{\kappa}=\vec{0}
, we denote it by
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]
.

Let
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be the group scheme
\mathrm{Res}_{C/\bar{C}}(G^{\prime}_{C})^{\Gamma}
. There is a natural morphism
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}
.
Let
\iota:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
be the morphism given by
(\vec{p},\mathcal{F},\beta)\mapsto(\vec{p},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\beta})
, where
\bar{\mathcal{F}}=\mathcal{F}\times^{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\mathcal{G}
and
\bar{\beta}
is the trivialization induced from
\beta
.

Lemma 5.16
.

The morphism
\iota:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
is a closed embedding.

Proof.

From
\iota
, we naturally get a morphism
\iota_{\vec{\lambda}}:\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
for any
\vec{\lambda}\in(X_{*}(T^{\prime})^{+})^{n}
. Over each point
\vec{p}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
, the morphism
\iota_{\vec{\lambda},\vec{p}}:\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\vec{p}}\to\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}
is an isomorphism, cf. Corollary
4.21
. By Lemma
5.12
,
\iota_{\vec{\lambda}}
must be an isomorphism. Taking the direct limit of
\iota_{\vec{\lambda}}
with respect to the standard partial order
\preceq
, by Proposition
4.25
we recover the map
\iota
. Thus,
\iota
is a closed embedding.
∎

From Lemma
5.16
, the morphism
\iota
restricts to an isomorphism
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}_{\xi}^{n}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}_{\xi}^{n}}[\vec{o}]
. Thus, we get an isomorphism

\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}].

For each
1\leq i\leq n
, let
z_{i}
be the
i
-th coordinates of
\mathbb{A}^{n}
. For any
\vec{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\in(X_{*}(T))^{n}
, we define

n_{\vec{\mu}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma}(t-\gamma^{-1}\cdot z_{i})^{\gamma(\mu_{i})}

to be a section
\mathbb{A}^{n}\to L\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, where
t
is the standard coordinate of
C
. Point-wisely, it sends
\vec{p}
to
\prod_{i,\gamma}(t-\gamma(p_{i}))^{\gamma(\mu_{i})}\in L\mathcal{G}_{\vec{p}}
. Let
\kappa_{i}
be the image of
\mu_{i}
in
X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}
, then
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{\vec{\mu}}\subseteq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
, and the translation by
n_{\vec{\mu}}
gives an isomorphism

(5.28)

T_{\vec{\mu}}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}].

Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
be the level one line bundle over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, which is constructed in Section
5.1
. It gives rise to a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]
via the isomorphism
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]
. Denote by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\mu}}:=(T_{\vec{\mu}})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
the push forward of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
via
T_{\vec{\mu}}
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Given any
\vec{\mu},\vec{\nu}\in\big(X_{*}(T)^{+}\big)^{n}
whose images in
\big(X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}\big)^{n}
are
\vec{\kappa}
, there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\nu}}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\mu}}
of line bundles over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
.

Proof.

Consider the isomorphism
T_{\vec{\nu}-\vec{\mu}}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]
. Regard
(T_{\vec{\nu}-\vec{\mu}})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
as line bundles over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Then they have rigidified structures automatically since any line bundle over
\mathbb{A}^{n}
is trivializable. Moreover, they have the same central charge at any point. By Theorem
5.8
,
(T_{\vec{\nu}-\vec{\mu}})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Pushing forward via
T_{\vec{\mu}}
, we get an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\nu}}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\mu}}
.
∎

From this lemma, the line bundle
(T_{\vec{\mu}})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
only depends on
\vec{\kappa}
. We shall denote it by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
.

Proposition 5.18
.

For any
\vec{\kappa}=(\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{n})\in\big(X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}\big)^{n}
, set
\kappa:=\sum\kappa_{i}
. Then

(1)

There is an isomorphism of line bundles over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}[\vec{\kappa}]

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}|_{\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{1},\kappa}.

(2)

Given a partition
\xi=(I,J)
of
[n]
, there is an isomorphism of line bundles over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},C_{\xi}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}\simeq\big(\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{I},\vec{\kappa}_{I}}\boxtimes\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{J},\vec{\kappa}_{J}}\big)|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}.

Proof.

(1) By Theorem
5.8
, the restriction of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}|_{\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}
to the diagonal
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. Thus,
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}|_{\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
as line bundles over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}[\vec{o}]\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}[o]
. For each
i
, choose
\mu_{i}\in X_{*}(T)
such that its image in
X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}
is
\kappa_{i}
. Set
\vec{\mu}=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{n})
and
\mu=\sum\mu_{i}
. Restricting the section
n_{\vec{\mu}}
to the diagonal, we get a map
\mathbb{A}^{1}\simeq\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\xrightarrow{n_{\vec{\mu}}}L\mathcal{G}_{\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}\simeq L\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
sending
p
to
\prod_{\gamma}(t-\gamma(p))^{\gamma(\sum\mu_{i})}
. Let
\mu=\sum\mu_{i}\in X_{*}(T)
, then this composition map coincides with the section
n_{\mu}:\mathbb{A}^{1}\to L\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
. Therefore,

\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}|_{\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}\simeq\big((T_{\vec{\mu}})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\big)|_{\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}\simeq(T_{\mu})_{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{1},\kappa}

as line bundles over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\Delta_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}}[\vec{\kappa}]
.

(2) It follows from the factorization properties of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
and
n_{\vec{\mu}}
.
∎

Proposition 5.19
.

For any
\vec{\kappa}\in\big(X_{*}(T)/\check{Q}\big)^{n}
, there is a unique
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
5.9
, the uniqueness is clear. We now prove the existence.
Choose
\vec{\mu}\in(X_{*}(T))^{n}
such that its image in
(X_{*}(T)/\check{Q})^{n}
is
\vec{\kappa}
. Recall that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
is the push forward of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
via
T_{\vec{\mu}}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
. Thus, an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
is equivalent to an
\mathrm{Ad}_{n_{-\vec{\mu}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]
. By the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, this is equivalent to an
\mathrm{Ad}_{n_{-\vec{\mu}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
-equivariant structure on the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.

Consider the morphism
\phi:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal{V}_{0})}
induced from the adjoint representation
\mathcal{G}\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal{V}_{0})
. One can define a determinant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal{V}_{0})}
by (
5.25
). Then the pullback
\phi^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
is a line bundle over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
with a natural
L^{+}\mathcal{G}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure, which will still be denoted by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}
. Restricting to the
\vec{\kappa}
-component, we get an
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]}
. This implies that
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]}
has an
\mathrm{Ad}_{n_{-\vec{\mu}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
-equivariant structure.
Via the identification
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]
, by Theorem
5.8
we have
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{det}}|_{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{o}]}\simeq(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})^{l}
for some
l
. Thus
(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})^{l}
is
\mathrm{Ad}_{n_{-\vec{\mu}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
-equivariant.

For any
\vec{\lambda}\in(X_{*}(T))^{n}
whose image in
(X_{*}(T)/\check{Q})^{n}
is
\vec{\kappa}
, by the same proof of Lemma
5.11
, one can show the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-action on
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\subseteq\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
factors through a finite type group scheme
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Let
{}_{\vec{\mu}}L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
denote
\mathrm{Ad}_{n_{-\vec{\mu}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
, and regard
{}_{\vec{\mu}}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}:=T_{\vec{\mu}}^{-1}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\big)
as a subscheme of
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Then the
{}_{\vec{\mu}}L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-action on
{}_{\vec{\mu}}\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
factors through a finite type group scheme
{}_{\vec{\mu}}L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Define
{}_{\vec{\mu}}\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
similarly as in (
5.20
), by the same argument as in Proposition
5.10
, one can show the following central extension splits

(5.29)

1\to Z_{l}\to_{\vec{\mu}}\overline{L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\xrightarrow{p}_{\vec{\mu}}L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to 1.

It follows that the line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
is
\mathrm{Ad}_{n_{-\vec{\mu}}}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
-equivariant. Equivalently,
the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
is
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
-equivariant.
∎

Definition 5.20
.

For any BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, it is contained in a unique
\vec{\kappa}
-component
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
. With no confusion, we will denote by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
on
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, and call it the level one line bundle over
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.

Corollary 5.21
.

The space
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\big)
is a
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-module.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.19
, the group scheme
L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
acts on
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\big)
. This induces an action of
{\rm Lie}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}’_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
on
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\big)
. Note that
L^{+}\mathcal{G}’_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\simeq\varprojlim L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}’_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, where
L_{k}^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
is defined in (
5.19
). Thus,

{\rm Lie}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})\simeq\varprojlim_{k}\mathfrak{g}\bigg(\mathbb{C}[t,z_{1},\cdots,z_{n}]/\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{\gamma\in\Gamma}(t-\gamma\cdot z_{i})^{k}\bigg)^{\Gamma}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{n}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}].

There is a natural morphism
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}\to{\rm Lie}(L^{+}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
induced from the natural inclusion
\mathbb{C}[t]\to\mathbb{C}[t,z_{1},\cdots,z_{n}]
. Therefore,
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\big)
is a
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-module.
∎

6.
Global Demazure modules and BD Schubert varieties

In this section, we first equip a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on level one line bundles on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Then, we prove a Borel-Weil type theorem on BD Schubert varieties for twisted global Demazure modules.

6.1.
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariance on the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}

Let
\sigma
be a standard automorphism
G
of order
m
as in Section
2.2
, and let
\Gamma
be the cyclic group generated by
\sigma
. Denote by
\mathbb{C}[t]
the coordinate ring of the affine space
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Recall the standard
\Gamma
-action on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
given by
\sigma(t)=\epsilon^{-1}t
. Let
\pi:\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
denote the quotient map sending
a
to
\bar{a}
. We can identify
\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
with
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Under this identification,
\pi
can be regarded as the map
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
given by
a\mapsto a^{m}
. Similarly, we can define the
\Gamma
-action on
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and the quotient space
\bar{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Let
\mathcal{G}=\mathrm{Res}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\big(G\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\big)^{\Gamma}
be the parahoric Bruhat-Tits group scheme over
\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
defined as in Section
4.1
. For any scheme
S
over
\mathbb{C}
, Set
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}:=\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S
and
\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{S}:=\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times S
. We denote by
\mathcal{G}_{S}
the group scheme
\mathcal{G}\times_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{S}
over
\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{S}
.
For any
h\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(S)
, let
\rho_{h}:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}
be the map sending
(a,s)
to
(h(s)a,s)
. It induces a map
\bar{\rho}_{h}:\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}\to\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}
sending
(\bar{a},s)
to
(\bar{h}(s)\bar{a},s)
, where
\bar{h}
is image of
h
via
\mathbb{G}_{m}(S)\to\bar{\mathbb{G}}_{m}(S)
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For any
h\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(S)
, there is an isomorphism of group schemes over
\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{S}

\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{S})\simeq\mathcal{G}_{S}.

As a consequence, the pullback
(\bar{\rho}_{h})^{*}\mathcal{F}
of a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}
over
\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times S
for any scheme
S
over
\mathbb{C}
, via
\bar{\rho}_{h}
is also a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor.

Proof.

For any scheme
T
over
\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}
, denote by
(\bar{\rho}_{h})_{*}T
its pull-forward via
\bar{\rho}_{h}
, i.e.
(\bar{\rho}_{h})_{*}T
is regarded as the scheme over
\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}
via the composition
T\to\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}\xrightarrow{\bar{\rho}_{h}}\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}
. There is a
\Gamma
-equivariant isomorphism
f:T\times_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}}\mathbb{A}_{S}^{1}\simeq((\bar{\rho}_{h})_{*}T)\times_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}}\mathbb{A}_{S}^{1}
of schemes, given by
(t,a)\mapsto(t,h^{m}(a))
. This induces an isomorphism of groups

(\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{S})(T)=\mathcal{G}_{S}\big((\bar{\rho}_{h})_{*}T\big)=G\Big(\big((\bar{\rho}_{h})_{*}T\big)\times_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}}\mathbb{A}_{S}^{1}\Big)^{\Gamma}\xrightarrow{f}G\big(T\times_{\bar{\mathbb{A}}_{S}^{1}}\mathbb{A}_{S}^{1}\big)^{\Gamma}=\mathcal{G}_{S}(T).

Thus,
\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{S})\simeq\mathcal{G}_{S}
.

For any
\mathcal{G}_{S}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}
over
\bar{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{S}
, the pull-back
\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}\mathcal{F}
is an
\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{S}
-torsor, and hence is a
\mathcal{G}_{S}
-torsor via isomorphism
\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}\mathcal{G}_{S}\simeq\mathcal{G}_{S}
.

∎

From the proof, one can see that this proposition also holds for
\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
. Let
\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}
be the moduli stack of
\mathcal{G}
-torsors on
\bar{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
. For any scheme
S
over
\mathbb{C}
and
h\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(S)
, we define a map
\phi_{h}:\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}(S)\to\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}(S)
, sending
\mathcal{F}
to
\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}\mathcal{F}
. This gives an
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}
.

By Lemma
6.1
, for any
h\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(S)
, one can define the following map

(6.1)

\displaystyle\psi_{h}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}(S)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}(S)

\displaystyle(p,\mathcal{F},\beta)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\,h\cdot p,\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}\mathcal{F},\bar{\rho}_{h}^{*}\beta\,).

Recall that
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},0}=\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}
, the restriction of
\psi_{h}
to the
0
-fiber induces a map
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}(S)\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}(S)
, which will still be denoted by
\psi_{h}
. This gives a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}
. In fact, one can check this action agrees with the action induced from the natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{C}[[t]]
sending
t
to
a^{-1}t
for any
a\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{C})
.

Now, we assume
G
is simply connected. By the uniformization theorem
[
22
]
, we have
\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}\simeq G[t^{-1}]^{\sigma}\backslash\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}
as algebraic stacks. Then, we have the following commutative diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.68407pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.46877pt\raise
