26.96101pt\raise-25.8889pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.68407pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.68407pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}(S)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Let
\mathscr{L}
be the level one line bundle on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathscr{G}}
. From
[
4
, Theorem 3.13]
, the line bundle has a natural
G[t^{-1}]^{\sigma}\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure. The
G[t^{-1}]^{\sigma}
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathscr{L}
descends to the ample generator
\mathcal{L}
of
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}})
. Moreover, Lemma
C.1
implies that
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathscr{L}
also descends to a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathcal{L}
.

Let
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
be the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian over
\mathbb{A}^{n}
. For
h\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
, we define a map
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
sending
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n},\mathcal{F},\beta)
to
(ha_{1},\ldots,ha_{n},(h^{m})^{*}\mathcal{F},(h^{m})^{*}\beta)
. It gives a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Recall that the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
is the pull-back of
\mathcal{L}
via the projection
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\mathrm{Bun}_{\mathcal{G}}
. Then the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathcal{L}
induces a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. One can check this action is compatible with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. In summary, we have the following result.

Lemma 6.2
.

When
G
is simply connected, there exists a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, which restricts to the natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\vec{p}}
over
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}
for any
\vec{p}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
. ∎

Let
G
be adjoint, and let
G^{\prime}
be its simply connected cover. With the same setup as in Section
5.3
, for any BD Schubert variety
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, it is contained in a unique
\vec{\kappa}
-component
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
. Recall Definition
5.20
, the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
is defined to be the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}
to
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

When
G
is adjoint, there exists a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, which restricts to the natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\vec{p}}
over
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}}
for any
\vec{p}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
.

Proof.

For any
h\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
, we define
\psi_{h}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{h}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
similarly as in (
6.1
). Clearly,
\psi_{h}
preserves the
\kappa
-component. Hence, it restricts to an morphism
\psi_{h}:\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]\to\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
. One can easily check that the following diagram commutes,

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.11351pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.11351pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.92949pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi^{\prime}_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.6946pt\raise 7.75833pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.02501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{T_{\vec{\lambda}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.11351pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.11351pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.99197pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi_{h}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.92146pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-20.11351pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.6946pt\raise-24.24167pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.02501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{T_{\vec{\lambda}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.11351pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.11351pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
T_{\vec{\lambda}}
is defined in (
5.28
). Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
be the level one line bundle over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. Then, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
induces a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\vec{\kappa}}:=(T_{\vec{\lambda}})_{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}[\vec{\kappa}]
. Restricting to
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
, we get a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. The second statement of the proposition directly follows from Lemma
6.2
.
∎

Theorem 6.4
.

With the same setup as in Proposition
6.3
, the space
H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
is a graded free
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-module.

Proof.

By Theorem
3.9
, Corollary
4.21
and the isomorphism (
4.36
), the function
\varphi(\vec{p}):=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\vec{p}},\mathcal{L}_{\vec{p}}\big)
is a constant function over
\vec{p}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
. Thus,
H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
is a projective
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-module.

Moreover, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
endows
H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
a graded structure, which is compatible with the natural grading of the polynomial ring
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
. Using the graded Nakayama lemma (cf.
[
35
,
Tag 0EKB
]
),
H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
is a graded free
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-module.
∎

6.2.
Borel-Weil type theorem for twisted global Demazure modules

With the same setup as in Proposition
6.3
, by Theorem
6.4
, the space
H^{0}(\mathrm{Gr}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})
is a graded free
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-module. Denote by
H^{0}(\mathrm{Gr}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})^{\vee}
its dual as a
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-module. Then,
H^{0}(\mathrm{Gr}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}})^{\vee}
is still a graded free
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-module, and is a
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-module by Corollary
5.21
.

In the following context, we will simply denote by
\mathcal{L}
the level one line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
over
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}
. By the factorization of Schubert varieties in Theorem
4.13
and the factorization of line bundles in Proposition
5.18
, we have an isomorphism

f_{\xi}:H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\simeq\Big(H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{1}}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{I_{1}}},\mathcal{L}^{c}_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{1}}}\big)^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{k}}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{I_{k}}},\mathcal{L}^{c}_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{k}}}\big)^{\vee}\Big)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}],

By Theorem
3.13
, the following
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-module

(6.2)

\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}:=\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})\otimes_{\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]

is a graded and free over
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
. Finally, we have the following theorem.

Theorem 6.5
.

For any
n>0
, there exists an isomorphism of
(\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma},\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}])
-bimodules:

(6.3)

\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}:\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\simeq H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}.

Moreover, for any nontrivial partition
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
of
[n]
, the following diagram commutes

(6.4)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 72.74269pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-53.27145pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-9.1111pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.12506pt\raise-17.99135pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi_{\xi}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.53824pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.42061pt\raise 7.57915pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.67085pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 170.26683pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 170.26683pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}_{\xi}^{n}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 197.82759pt\raise-16.27504pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{{}{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{\xyRotate@@{1024}\kern-1.78072pt\kern 1.78072pt}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 206.51613pt\raise-17.99135pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f_{\xi}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 206.51613pt\raise-23.23509pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-72.74269pt\raise-35.9827pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Big(\bigotimes_{j}\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,{\vec{\lambda}}_{I_{j}})_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{j}}}\Big)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.61769pt\raise-31.63841pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.56621pt\raise-45.10774pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.87505pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\prod\Phi_{j})|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.74269pt\raise-35.9827pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 120.74269pt\raise-35.9827pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Big(\bigotimes_{j}H^{0}\Big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}_{I_{j}}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{I_{j}}},\mathcal{L}^{c}_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{j}}}\Big)^{\vee}\Big)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\vec{\lambda}_{I_{j}}
is defined in (
3.22
),
\Phi_{j}:=\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{j}}}
, and the map
\psi_{\xi}
is defined in (
3.23
) in Proposition
3.18
. As a consequence,
\psi_{\xi}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

When
n=1
, set
\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}=\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}
. We have an isomorphism
\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}\simeq\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{G}\times\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, cf.
[
39
, Lemma 3.4.2]
. Thus, there is an
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-isomorphism

H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\simeq D(c,\lambda)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}],

where the
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-action on two sides are given by Corollary
5.21
and equation (
3.2
) respectively.
Composing with the map
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}
, we get an embedding of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-modules

(6.5)

H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\hookrightarrow D(c,\lambda)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}].

Recall Proposition
3.18
that there is an embedding of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-modules

(6.6)

\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\hookrightarrow D(c,\lambda)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}].

Taking the lowest weight parts of these modules in (
6.5
) and (
6.6
) with respect to the
\mathfrak{h}^{\sigma}
-action, we get the following embeddings:

\displaystyle\phi:H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\hookrightarrow D(c,\lambda)_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}],

\displaystyle\psi:(\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\hookrightarrow D(c,\lambda)_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{C}[\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}].

We shall show
\mathrm{Im}(\phi)=\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
. Note that
\mathrm{Im}(\phi)
and
\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
are free rank one
\mathbb{C}[z]
-modules, and
D(c,\lambda)_{c\iota(\lambda)}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathring{\mathbb{A}}^{1}]
is a free rank one
\mathbb{C}[z,z^{-1}]
-module. We must have

\mathrm{Im}(\phi)=z^{a}\mathrm{Im}(\psi).

We claim
a=0
. By Theorem
6.4
and Theorem
3.13
,
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}
and
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
are graded free
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{1}]
-modules. By Theorem
3.12
and Corollary
4.21
, we can compute their characters via the fibers at
0\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
:

(6.7)

\mathrm{ch}\big(H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\big)=\mathrm{ch}\big(D^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)\big)\cdot(1-q)^{-1}=\mathrm{ch}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\big).

In particular, on the lowest weight part, we have

\mathrm{ch}\big(H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\big)=\mathrm{ch}\big((\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\big).

Hence
\mathrm{ch}\big(\mathrm{Im}(\phi)\big)=\mathrm{ch}\big(\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\big)
. It follows that
a=0
. In other words,
\mathrm{Im}(\phi)=\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
, and furthermore

H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\simeq(\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})_{-c\iota(\lambda)}.

Consider the following embedding of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-modules

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}=\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma})\mathbin{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.6}{\textbullet}}}(\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})_{-c\iota(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{U}(\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma})\cdot H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}_{-c\iota(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\lambda},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}.

By the equality (
6.7
) of characters, this embedding must be an isomorphism, which will be denoted by
\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
.

When
n=2
, let
\xi=(\{1\},\{2\})
be the nontrivial partition. From the case when
n=1
, we have an isomorphism
(f_{\xi})^{-1}\circ(\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\times\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})|_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}}
of
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}]
-modules

(6.8)

\big(\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,{\lambda}_{1})_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\otimes\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,{\lambda}_{2})_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\big)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{2}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}]\simeq H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}.

Combining with Proposition
3.18
, we get an embedding

(6.9)

\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}.

Consider the restriction map

(6.10)

H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}.

Taking the lowest weight parts of these modules in (
6.10
) and (
6.9
) with respect to the
\mathfrak{g}^{\sigma}
-action, we get the following embeddings:

\displaystyle\phi:H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)_{-c\iota(\lambda)}^{\vee},

\displaystyle\psi:(\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{2}})_{-c\iota(\lambda)}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)_{-c\iota(\lambda)}^{\vee}.

We shall show
\mathrm{Im}(\phi)=\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
. Note that
\mathrm{Im}(\phi)
and
\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
are free rank one
\mathbb{C}[z_{1},z_{2}]
-modules, and
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)_{-c\iota(\lambda)}^{\vee}

is a free rank one
\mathbb{C}[z_{1},z_{2}]_{\mathfrak{a}}
-modules, where
\mathfrak{a}
is the ideal generated by
z_{1}-\epsilon^{i}z_{2}
, with
i=0,\cdots,m-1
. We must have

(6.11)

\mathrm{Im}(\phi)=\prod_{i=0}^{m-1}(z_{1}-\epsilon^{i}z_{2})^{a_{i}}\mathrm{Im}(\psi).

We claim
a_{i}=0
for any
i
. Consider the
\Gamma
-action on
\mathbb{A}^{2}
defined by
\sigma*(x,y):=(x,\epsilon(y))
. This action extends to an action on
\mathrm{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}}
. From the construction of
\mathcal{L}
(cf. (
5.4
) and Section
5.3
), there is a natural
\Gamma
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}
. When
n=1
, it is clear that
\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is equivariant under the standard action of
\Gamma
defined in (
4.37
). Hence, the morphism (
6.8
) is
\Gamma
-equivariant. Then the induced map (
6.9
) and hence
\psi
is
\Gamma
-equivariant.
From (
6.11
), the
\Gamma
-equivariance of
\phi
and
\psi
imply that

\textstyle\prod_{i}(z_{1}-\epsilon^{i-1}z_{2})^{a_{i}}=\prod_{i}(z_{1}-\epsilon^{i}z_{2})^{a_{i}}.

It follows that
a_{i}=a_{i-1}
for any
i
. Since
\mathrm{ch}\big(\mathrm{Im}(\phi)\big)=\mathrm{ch}\big(\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\big)
, we must have
\sum a_{i}=0
. Therefore,
a_{i}=0
and
\mathrm{Im}(\phi)=\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
. By the same argument as in the case
n=1
, this leads to an isomorphism
\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}:\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\simeq H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{2}}^{\vec{\lambda}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}
. From the construction,
\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}
satisfies diagram (
6.4
).

When
n>2
, we prove it by induction. Let
\xi=(I_{1},I_{2},\ldots,I_{k})
be any nontrivial partition, i.e.
\xi\neq([n])
.
By induction, we get an embedding

\textstyle\Phi_{\xi}:=f_{\xi}^{-1}\circ(\prod\Phi_{\alpha})|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}\circ\psi_{\xi}:\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}.

Let
\xi^{\prime}=(I_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,I_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime})
be another nontrivial partition. Consider a new partition as follows (with the lexicographical order),

\xi^{\prime\prime}:=(I_{\alpha}\cap I^{\prime}_{\beta}\mid 1\leq\alpha\leq k,1\leq\beta\leq k^{\prime}).

It is easy to check
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}=\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime}}
. For any
1\leq\alpha\leq k
, we denote by
\xi^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}
the partition
(I_{\alpha}\cap I^{\prime}_{\beta}\mid 1\leq\beta\leq k^{\prime})
of
I_{\alpha}
. By induction, we have
\Phi_{\alpha}|_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{\alpha}}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}}}=f_{\xi_{\alpha}}^{-1}\circ(\prod_{\beta}\Phi_{\alpha,\beta})|_{\mathbb{A}^{I_{\alpha}}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}}}\circ\psi_{\xi_{\alpha}}
over
\mathbb{A}^{I_{\alpha}}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}}
for any
1\leq\alpha\leq k
. It follows that

\displaystyle\textstyle\Phi_{\xi}|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle\textstyle=f_{\xi}^{-1}\circ(\prod_{\alpha}\Phi_{\alpha})|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}}\circ\psi_{\xi}

\displaystyle\textstyle=f_{\xi}^{-1}\circ(\prod_{\alpha}f_{\xi^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}}^{-1})|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}}\circ(\prod_{\alpha,\beta}\Phi_{\alpha,\beta})|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}}\circ(\prod_{\alpha}\psi_{\xi^{\prime\prime}_{\alpha}})|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}}\circ\psi_{\xi}

\displaystyle\textstyle=f_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}\circ(\prod_{\alpha,\beta}\Phi_{\alpha,\beta})|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}}\circ\psi_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}.

Similarly
\Phi_{\xi^{\prime}}|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}}=\Phi_{\xi^{\prime\prime}}
. Thus,

\Phi_{\xi}
and
\Phi_{\xi^{\prime}}
agree on
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi^{\prime}}
. Hence, we can glue these
\{\Phi_{\xi}\}_{\xi}
together to get an embedding over
\bigcup_{\xi}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}=\mathbb{A}^{n}-\Delta_{[n]}
, i.e.

\Phi:\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}-\Delta_{[n]}]\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}-\Delta_{[n]}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee},

where
\Delta_{[n]}
is defined in (
4.40
). Since
\Delta_{[n]}
has dimension
1
, by Hartogs’ Lemma,
\Phi
extends to an embedding over
\mathbb{A}^{n}
,

\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}:\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}\hookrightarrow H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\vec{\lambda}}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{n}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big)^{\vee}.

Note that both sides are graded free
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{n}]
-modules, and have the same graded
T^{\sigma}
-character as the same argument as in the cases for
n=1,2
. Thus,
\Phi_{\mathbb{A}^{n}}
must be an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 6.6
.

(1)

The proof of this theorem is similar to the method used in
[
10
, Section 5.1]
. It is worth noting that, in the twisted case, it is already nontrivial when
n=1
, and when
n=2
the argument is more subtle. Moreover, in our argument, in order to prove the isomorphism (
6.3
), the commutativity of the diagram (
6.4
) needs to be involved in the induction.

(2)

In
[
10
]
, a version of Theorem
6.5
is proved for symmetric BD Schubert varieties in the untwisted case. In our paper, we only work with the twisted BD Schubert varieties over
\mathbb{A}^{n}
, where the coordinates of points are ordered. The main reason is that the algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})
is more complicated than the untwisted case, see Remark
3.23
. In particular, when
n=1
,
H^{0}\big(\overline{\mathrm{Gr}}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{G},\mathbb{A}^{1}},\mathcal{L}^{c}\big))
is naturally an
\mathbb{C}[z]
-module, while
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\lambda)
is a
\mathbb{C}[z^{r}]
-module for some integer
r\geq 1
, where
r
depends on whether
\lambda
is
\sigma
-invariant, see Example
3.3
.

The following corollary immediately follows from Theorem
6.5
.

Corollary 6.7
.

Let
\vec{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n})\in(X_{*}(T)^{+})^{n}
.

(1)

Let
\xi=(I_{1},\ldots,I_{k})
be a partition on
[n]
. For any point
\vec{p}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\xi}
, there is an isomorphism of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-modules as follows,

\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\vec{p}}=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda}_{I_{j}})_{\vec{p}_{I_{j}}},

where
\vec{p}_{I_{j}}
and
\vec{\lambda}_{I_{j}}
are defined in (
3.21
) and (
3.22
).

(2)

For any
\vec{p}=(p,\gamma_{2}(p),\ldots,\gamma_{n}(p))\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
such that
p\neq 0
, there is an isomorphism of
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-modules

\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\vec{p}}=D(c,\lambda_{\vec{p}})_{p},

where
\lambda_{\vec{p}}:=\lambda_{1}+\gamma^{-1}_{2}(\lambda_{2})+\cdots+\gamma_{n}^{-1}(\lambda_{n})
and
D(c,\lambda_{\vec{p}})_{p}
denotes the module
D(c,\lambda_{\vec{p}})
with a new
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-action given by (
3.8
).

Proof.

Part (1) follows from the fact that the
\mathfrak{g}[t]^{\sigma}
-morphism
\psi_{\xi}
defined in Proposition
3.18
is an isomorphism. Part (2) follows from Corollary
4.21
and Theorem
6.5
.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

Note that when
\vec{p}=(0,\cdots,0)\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
, the fiber
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\vec{p}}
is already determined in Theorem
3.12
, and this fact is used in the proof of Theorem
6.5
. This fact together with Corollary
6.7
determines the fiber
\mathbb{D}^{\sigma}(c,\vec{\lambda})_{\vec{p}}
at any point
\vec{p}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}
.

Appendix A
Tannakian interpretation for
(\Gamma,G)
-torsors

Let
G
be an affine algebraic group over an algebraically closed field
k
.
Recall a classical result that a
G
-torsors is equivalent to a faithful exact tensor functor from
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
to the category of locally free sheaves, cf.
[
29
,
6
]
. In this appendix, we generalize this equivalence to an equivariant setting.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group acting on a
k
-scheme
Y
and acting on an affine algebraic group
G
over
k
.

Definition A.1
.

A
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor is a scheme
\mathcal{F}
which is faithfully flat and affine over
Y
, and equipped with a
\Gamma
-action and a right
G
-action such that

(1)

The map
\mathcal{F}\to Y
is both
\Gamma
-equivariant and
G
-equivariant.

(2)

The
G
-action on
\mathcal{F}
is compatible with the
\Gamma
-action, i.e. the
G
-action map
\mathcal{F}\times G\to\mathcal{F}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant, where
\Gamma
-action on
\mathcal{F}\times G
is given by
\gamma(p,g)=(\gamma p,\gamma g)
for
\gamma\in\Gamma
.

(3)

The map
\mathcal{F}\times G\to\mathcal{F}\times_{Y}\mathcal{F}
sending
(p,g)
to
(p,pg)
is an isomorphism.

Given
(\Gamma,G)
-torsors
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, a
(\Gamma,G)
-equivariant map
f:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F^{\prime}}
is a morphism of
G
-torsors which is also

\Gamma
-equivariant.

We denote by
\mathbf{Bun}_{\Gamma,G}(Y)
the category of
(\Gamma,G)
-torsors over
Y
in which the morphisms are
(\Gamma,G)
-equivariant maps.

Definition A.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
,
\mathfrak{D}
be two tensor categories, and let
F,F^{\prime}:\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{D}
be two tensor functors. A morphism
f:F\to F^{\prime}
of tensor functors is a natural transformation from
F
to
F^{\prime}
such that, for any finite families
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
of objects in
\mathfrak{C}
, the diagram

(A.1)

\textstyle{\bigotimes_{i\in I}F(V_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigotimes_{i\in I}f_{V_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{F\big(\bigotimes_{i\in I}V_{i}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{f_{\bigotimes_{i\in I}V_{i}}}
\textstyle{\bigotimes_{i\in I}F^{\prime}(V_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}\big(\bigotimes_{i\in I}V_{i}\big)}

commutes. (See
[
12
, Definition 1.12]
)

Remark A.3
.

This definition is equivalent to the commutativity of the diagram (
A.1
) for
I=\{1,2\}
and
I=\emptyset
.

Denote by
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
the category of finite dimensional algebraic representations of
G
over
k
, and by
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
the category of locally free sheaves of finite rank on
Y
. For each
\gamma\in\Gamma
, denote by
\gamma_{G}
(resp.
\gamma_{Y}
) the automorphism on
G
(resp.
Y
) associated to
\gamma
. Let
\gamma_{Y}^{*}:\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
be the pullback functors induced from
\gamma_{Y}
. We define a functor
\gamma_{G}^{*}:\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Rep}(G)
as follows, for any
(V,\rho)\in\mathrm{Rep}(G)
,

\gamma_{G}^{*}(V,\rho):=(V,\rho\circ\gamma_{G}).

The natural tensor structure on the category
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
(resp.
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
) is preserved by
\gamma^{*}_{G}
(resp.
\gamma_{Y}^{*}
).

Definition A.4
.

We say a tensor functor
F:\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
is
\Gamma
-equivariant if it is equipped with a collection of isomorphisms between tensor functors
\{\theta_{\gamma}:F\gamma_{G}^{*}\to\gamma_{Y}^{*}F\}_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
such that, for any
\eta,\gamma\in\Gamma
, the following diagram

(A.2)

\textstyle{F\eta_{G}^{*}\gamma_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F\delta}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\eta}}
\textstyle{\eta_{Y}^{*}F\gamma_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{*}\theta_{\gamma}}
\textstyle{\eta_{Y}^{*}\gamma_{Y}^{*}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{F(\gamma\eta)_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\gamma\eta}}
\textstyle{(\gamma\eta)_{Y}^{*}F}

commutes, where
\delta:\eta_{G}^{*}\gamma_{G}^{*}\simeq(\gamma\eta)_{G}^{*}
and
\xi:\eta_{Y}^{*}\gamma_{Y}^{*}\simeq(\gamma\eta)_{Y}^{*}
are natural isomorphisms between these functors. In fact, the commutativity of this diagram implies that
\theta_{e}=\mathrm{id}:F=Fe^{*}\to e^{*}F=F
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
G
-torsor over
Y
. For each object
V
in
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
, we denote by
\mathscr{F}_{V}
the sheaf of sections of the vector bundle
\mathcal{F}\times^{G}V
over
Y
. Then,
\mathscr{F}_{V}
is locally free and it is well-known that

\mathrm{Spec}\,S(\check{\mathscr{F}}_{V})\simeq\mathcal{F}\times^{G}V,

where
S(\check{\mathscr{F}}_{V})
denotes the symmetric algebra of the dual sheaf
\check{\mathscr{F}}_{V}
of
\mathscr{F}_{V}
, cf.
[
21
, Exercise 5.18]
. There is a natural functor

\Phi(\mathcal{F}):\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
associated to
\mathcal{F}
, which sends any representation
V
to the locally free sheaf
\mathscr{F}_{V}
on
Y
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor over
Y
. The functor
\Phi(\mathcal{F}):\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
is a faithful exact tensor functor and it is
\Gamma
-equivariant.

Proof.

By
[
6
, Lemma 4.1]
,
F
is a faithful exact tensor functor. Thus, it suffices to show
F
satisfies Definition
A.4
. Let
\theta_{\gamma}
be the natural isomorphism between the vector bundles
\mathcal{F}\times^{G}\gamma_{G}^{*}V
and
\gamma_{Y}^{*}(\mathcal{F}\times^{G}V)
over
Y
sending
(p,v)
to
(\gamma p,v)
. It is easy to check the collection of isomorphisms
\{\theta_{\gamma}\}_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
satisfies
A.4
.
∎

Definition A.6
.

Let
F
,
F^{\prime}
be two
\Gamma
-equivariant tensor functors from
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
to
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
. A morphism from
F
to
F^{\prime}
as
\Gamma
-equivariant tensor functors is a morphism
f:F\to F^{\prime}
of tensor functors such that for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
, the following diagram commutes

(A.3)

\textstyle{F\gamma_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{f\circ\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}\gamma_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{\prime}_{\gamma}}
\textstyle{\gamma_{Y}^{*}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\circ f}
\textstyle{\gamma_{Y}^{*}F^{\prime}}

Denote by
\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)
the category of tensor functors from
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
to
\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
that are faithful, exact and
\Gamma
-equivariant. The morphisms between these objects are defined to be morphisms between
\Gamma
-equivariant tensor functors.

Recall that given a
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor
\mathcal{F}
over
Y
, for each object
V
in
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
, one can associate a locally free sheaf
\mathscr{F}_{V}
. By Lemma
A.5
, the functor
\Phi(\mathcal{F}):\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
is an object in
\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)
. Let
f:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be a morphism of
(\Gamma,G)
-torsors.
We naturally get a morphism
\Phi(f):\Phi(\mathcal{F})\to\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
of
\Gamma
-equivariant tensor functors.
As a summary, we have the following result.

Proposition A.7
.

\Phi
is a functor from
\mathbf{Bun}_{\Gamma,G}(Y)
to
\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)
. ∎

Conversely, for any object
F
in
\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)
, we would like to construct a
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor corresponding to
F
.

Definition A.8
.

An affine
G
-scheme over
Y
is a scheme affine and flat over
Y
with a left
G
-action. Moreover, an affine
(\Gamma,G)
-scheme over
Y
is an affine
G
-scheme
f:X\to Y
over
Y
equipped with a
\Gamma
-action such that

(1)

f:X\to Y
is
\Gamma
-equivariant.

(2)

The
G
-action map
G\times X\to X
is
\Gamma
-equivariant.

Denote by
\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}(G)
the category of locally finite dimensional representations of
G
, and by
\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)
the category of quasi-coherent sheaves on
Y
. Let
F:\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
be a faithful exact tensor functor. One can extend it uniquely to a faithful exact tensor functor
\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}(G)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)
which will still be denoted by
F
, see
[
6
, Lemma 4.4]
. For an affine
G
-scheme
X
over
k
, we define a scheme over
Y

\tilde{F}(X):=\mathrm{Spec}\,F(k[X]),

where
k[X]
denotes the coordinate ring of
X
.
It was shown in
[
6
, Lemma 4.5]
that
\tilde{F}(X)
is flat and affine over
Y
. Thus,
\tilde{F}
gives to a functor from the category of affine
G
-schemes over
k
to the category of schemes affine and flat over
Y
. Given any representation
V
in
{\rm Rep}(G)
, it can be regarded as a
G
-scheme. Then
\tilde{F}(V)=\tilde{F(V)}
, where
\tilde{F(V)}
represents the affine bundle associated to the locally free sheaf
F(V)
.

Let
X
be an affine
G
-scheme over
k
and for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
, we define
\gamma_{G}^{*}X
to be the scheme
X
with a left
G
-action twisted by the morphism
\gamma_{G}:G\to G
, and define
\gamma_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}X
to be the pullback of the scheme
\tilde{F}X
via the morphism
\gamma_{Y}:Y\to Y
. Then we have
\gamma_{G}^{*}X=\mathrm{Spec}(\gamma_{G}^{*}(k[X]))
and
\gamma_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}X=\mathrm{Spec}(\gamma_{Y}^{*}F(k[X]))
. If
F:\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
is
\Gamma
-equivariant, then the extended functor
F:\mathrm{Rep}^{\infty}(G)\to\mathrm{QCoh}(Y)
is also
\Gamma
-equivariant, i.e. there is a collection of natural isomorphisms between tensor functors
\{\theta_{\gamma}:F\gamma_{G}^{*}\to\gamma_{Y}^{*}F\}_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
satisfying the diagram (
A.2
) in Definition
A.4
. For each
\gamma\in\Gamma
,
\theta_{\gamma}
induces an isomorphism
\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma,X}:\tilde{F}\gamma_{G}^{*}X\simeq\gamma_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}X
of schemes which is functorial in
X
.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
F:\mathrm{Rep}(G)\to\mathrm{Coh}^{\mathrm{lf}}(Y)
be a
\Gamma
-equivariant faithful and exact tensor functor. The induced functor
\tilde{F}
satisfies the following properties:

(1)

\tilde{F}
respects products and if
X
has a trivial
G
-action then
\tilde{F}(X)=Y\times X
with the obvious
G
-action.

(2)

For any
\gamma\in\Gamma
and any two affine
G
-schemes
X_{1},X_{2}
over
k
, the following diagram commutes

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 42.9224pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.17387pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{F}\gamma_{G}^{*}(X_{1}\times 58.27235pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\gamma_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}(X_{1})\times_{Y}\gamma_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}(X_{2})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

(3)

For any
\eta,\gamma\in\Gamma
, the following diagram

\textstyle{\tilde{F}\eta_{G}^{*}\gamma_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{F}\delta}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\theta}_{\eta}}
\textstyle{\eta_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}\gamma_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{*}\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma}}
\textstyle{\eta_{Y}^{*}\gamma_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\xi}
\textstyle{\tilde{F}(\gamma\eta)_{G}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma\eta}}
\textstyle{(\gamma\eta)_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}}

commutes, where
\delta:\eta_{G}^{*}\gamma_{G}^{*}\simeq(\gamma\eta)_{G}^{*}
and
\xi:\eta_{Y}^{*}\gamma_{Y}^{*}\simeq(\gamma\eta)_{Y}^{*}
are the natural isomorphisms between functors.

Proof.

Part (1) was established in
[
6
, Lemma 4.5]
. Part (2) follows from that fact that
\theta_{\gamma}
is a morphism of tensor functors. Part (3) follows from the commutativity (
A.2
) for
\{\theta_{\gamma}\}_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
.
∎

Definition A.10
.

An affine
\Gamma
-scheme
X
over
Y
is a scheme affine and flat over
Y
with a
\Gamma
-action such that the map
X\to Y
is
\Gamma
-equivariant.

Corollary A.11
.

With the same assumption as in Lemma
A.9
,
F
induces a functor
\tilde{F}
from the category of affine
(\Gamma,G)
-schemes over
k
to the category of affine
\Gamma
-schemes over
Y
.

Proof.

Let
X
be any
(\Gamma,G)
-scheme over
k
. For any
\gamma\in\Gamma
, the map
X\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\gamma_{G}^{*}X
sending
x
to
\gamma(x)
is an isomorphism of
G
-schemes. Applying the functor
\tilde{F}
, we get the following isomorphism of
G
-schemes over
Y

\varphi_{\gamma}:\tilde{F}X\mathrel{\!\tilde{\,\to}}\tilde{F}\gamma_{G}^{*}X\xrightarrow{\tilde{\theta}_{\gamma,X}}\gamma^{*}_{Y}\tilde{F}X.

Moreover, for any
\eta,\gamma\in\Gamma
, the following diagram commutes

is the natural isomorphism between functors. This amounts to a

-action on

.
∎

Denote by
G_{0}
the underlying scheme of the algebraic group
G
with the trivial right
G
-action. Let
a:G\times G_{0}\to G
be the map sending
(g_{1},g_{2})
to
g_{1}g_{2}
. Applying
\tilde{F}
and by part (1) of Lemma
A.9
, this gives rise to a map

\tilde{a}:\tilde{F}G\times G=\tilde{F}G\times_{Y}\tilde{F}G_{0}\to\tilde{F}G.

It was shown in
[
6
, Lemma 4.7]
that
\tilde{a}
gives a right
G
-action on
\tilde{F}G
, and furthermore
\tilde{F}G
is a
G
-torsor over
Y
. In fact, we have more.

Proposition A.12
.

\tilde{F}G
is a
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor over
Y
.

Proof.

By Corollary
A.11
,
\tilde{F}G
is a
\Gamma
-scheme over
Y
.
Also, as discussed above,
\tilde{F}G
is a right
G
-torsor. Thus, it suffices to show that
\tilde{a}:\tilde{F}G\times G\to\tilde{F}G
is
\Gamma
-equivariant. Since
G
is a

-scheme, we have the following commutative diagram of

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.56062pt\raise-15.86111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.52084pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\gamma}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 65.56062pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-25.80147pt\raise-31.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\gamma^{*}_{Y}\tilde{F}G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.02368pt\raise-25.11111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.61111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{a}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.80147pt\raise-31.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.80147pt\raise-31.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\gamma_{Y}^{*}\tilde{F}G}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

This implies that the map
\tilde{a}:\tilde{F}G\times G\to\tilde{F}G
is
\Gamma
-equivariant.
∎

Given a morphism
\beta:F_{1}\to F_{2}
in
\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)
, one can further show there is a morphism
\tilde{\beta}_{G}:\tilde{F}_{1}G\to\tilde{F}_{2}G
between two
(\Gamma,G)
-torsors over
Y
. Define
\Psi(F):=\tilde{F}G
and
\Psi(\beta):=\tilde{\beta}_{G}
. This gives a functor

\Psi:\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)\to\mathbf{Bun}_{\Gamma,G}(Y).

Theorem A.13
.

The functor
\Psi:\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)\to\mathbf{Bun}_{\Gamma,G}(Y)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We will show
\Psi
is inverse to the functor
\Phi
, which is well-defined by Proposition
A.7
. Let
\pi:\mathcal{F}\to Y
be a
(\Gamma,G)
-torsor over
Y
, we have
\Psi\Phi(\mathcal{F})=\mathrm{Spec}\,(\mathscr{F}_{k[G]})
, where
\mathscr{F}_{k[G]}
denotes the sheaf of sections of
\mathcal{F}\times^{G}k[G]
over
Y
. We would like to show
\mathscr{F}_{k[G]}\simeq\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{F}}
, where
\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{F}}
denotes the structure sheaf of
\mathcal{F}
. For any open subset
U
of
Y
, let
\mathcal{F}_{U}
denote the
G
-torsor
\pi^{-1}(U)
over
U
. Each section
s:U\to\mathcal{F}_{U}\times^{G}k[G]
uniquely corresponds to a section:

\mathcal{F}_{U}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{U}\times_{U}U\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times s}\mathcal{F}_{U}\times_{U}\mathcal{F}_{U}\times^{G}k[G]\simeq(\mathcal{F}_{U}\times G)\times^{G}k[G]\simeq\mathcal{F}_{U}\times k[G].

This is equivalent to a
G
-equivariant map
\phi_{s}:\mathcal{F}_{U}\to k[G]
. Composing with
\mathrm{ev}_{e}:k[G]\to k
, we get an morphism
\mathrm{ev}_{e}\circ\phi_{s}:\mathcal{F}_{U}\to k
. Conversely, for any morphism
f:\mathcal{F}_{U}\to k
, there is a
G
-equivariant map
\tilde{f}:\mathcal{F}_{U}\to k[G]
defined by
\tilde{f}(p):g\mapsto f(pg)
. By the arguement above, this uniquely corresponds a section
U\to\mathcal{F}_{U}\times^{G}k[G]
. Thus, we have a
1
-
1
correspondence between the sections
U\to\mathcal{F}_{U}\times^{G}k[G]
and the morphisms
\mathcal{F}_{U}\to k
. Thus,
\mathscr{F}_{k[G]}\simeq\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{F}}
. One may further check this natural isomorphism is
(\Gamma,G)
-equivariant.
It follows that
\Psi\Phi(\mathcal{F})=\mathrm{Spec}\,(\mathscr{F}_{k[G]})\simeq\mathrm{Spec}\,(\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{F}})=\mathcal{F}
.

Conversely, given any object
F\in\mathbf{TF}_{\Gamma}(G,Y)
, we will show there is an isomorphism
\Phi\Psi(F)\to F
between
\Gamma
-equivariant tensor functors.
For each object
V
in
\mathrm{Rep}(G)
, by definition
\Phi\Psi(F)(V)
is the sheaf of sections of
\tilde{F}G\times^{G}V
over
Y
. Observe that
F(V)
is the sheaf of sections of
\tilde{F}(V)
over
Y
. Thus, it suffices to show there are
\Gamma
-equivariant maps
\{\tilde{F}G\times^{G}V\to\tilde{F}(V)\mid V\in\mathrm{Rep}(G)\}
functorial in
V
. Denote by
V_{0}
(resp.
G_{0}
) the scheme
V
(resp.
G
) with a trivial
G
-action. Consider the diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.48744pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.48744pt\raise V_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.02672pt\raise-15.66666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.85332pt\raise 5.84723pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.01389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times a}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.48744pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.87416pt\raise-26.82637pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{a}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.77283pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern where
a:G\times V_{0}\to V
sends
(g,v)
to
gv
, and
m:G\times G_{0}\to G
sends
(g_{1},g_{2})
to
g_{1}g_{2}
. Applying
\tilde{F}
, it induces an isomorphism
\tilde{a}:\tilde{F}G\times^{G}V\to\tilde{F}V
, cf.
[
6
, Theorem 4.8]
. From the construction of
\tilde{a}
, one may check
\tilde{a}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant and functorial in
V
.
∎

Appendix B
A generalization of cohomology and base change theorem

Definition B.1
.

An additive functor
T:\mathfrak{C}\to\mathfrak{D}
between two additive abelian categories is called half-exact if for any short exact sequence
0\to M\to N\to L\to 0
in
\mathfrak{C}
the sequence
T(M)\to T(N)\to T(L)
is exact.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a local homomorphism of Noetherian local rings. Let
\mathfrak{m}
be the maximal ideal of
A
, and
k=A/\mathfrak{m}
. Let
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(A)
(resp.
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(B)
) denote the category of finitely generated
A
-modules (resp.
B
-modules). Given an
A
-linear functor
T:\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(A)\to\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(B)
, one can attach a natural transformation

T(A)\otimes_{A}\cdot\to T.

This is constructed as follows. For any
A
-module
M
and any element
m\in M
, they give rise to an
A
-morphism
\phi_{m}:A\to M
, given by
a\mapsto am
. Applying
T
, we get
T(\phi_{m}):T(A)\to T(M)
. This induces an
A
-bilinear map
T(A)\times M\to T(M)
given by
(t,m)\mapsto T(\phi_{m})(t)
. Thus, this induces a map
T(A)\otimes_{A}M\to T(M)
. One may check this map is functorial in
M
.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
T
be a half-exact
A
-linear functor from
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(A)
to
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(B)
. Assume that
T
commutes with direct limits. Then,

(1)

If
T(k)=0
, then
T(M)=0
for any
M\in\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(A)
.

(2)

The following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

T(A)\to T(k)
is surjective.

(b)

The functor
T
is right exact.

(c)

T(A)\otimes_{A}\cdot\to T
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

cf.
[
30
, Theorem 2.2, Theorem 4.1]
.
∎

The following theorem is a generalization of cohomology and base change theorem, cf.
[
21
, Theorem 12.11]
.

Theorem B.3
.

Let
\pi:X\to Y
be a proper morphism of schemes over a Noetherian scheme
S
. Let
s\in S
, and let
X_{s}
(resp.
Y_{s}
) be the schematic fiber
X\times_{S}\mathrm{Spec}\,k(s)
(resp.
Y\times_{S}\mathrm{Spec}\,k(s)
), where
k(s)
is the residue field of
s
; let
\pi_{s}:X_{s}\to Y_{s}
be the induced morphism from
\pi
. Let
\mathscr{F}
be the coherent sheaf, which is flat over
S
. Then,

(1)

If
R^{i}\pi_{*}\mathscr{F}|_{X_{s}}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{i}(s)}R^{i}(\pi_{s})_{*}\mathscr{F}|_{X_{s}}
is surjective, then
\varphi^{i}(s)
is an isomorphism and it is true in a neighborhood of
s
in
S
.

(2)

Assume that
\varphi^{i}(s)
is surjective. Then the following are equivalant:

(a)

\varphi^{i-1}(s)
is surjective.

(b)

R^{i}\pi_{*}(\mathscr{F})
is flat in a neighborhood of
s
in
S
.

Proof.

Since the statements of the theorem are local, we may assume that
Y=\mathrm{Spec}B
,
S=\mathrm{Spec}A
, and the morphism
Y\to S
is given by a local homomorphism
A\to B
of local rings, where
A
is Noetherian. We consider the functor

T^{i}(M):=R^{i}\pi_{*}(X,\mathscr{F}\otimes_{A}M).

Then
T^{i}
is a half-exact
A
-linear functor from
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(A)
to
\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{fg}}(B)
, as
\mathscr{F}
is flat over
S
and
\pi
is proper. Part (1) immediately follows from part (2) of Proposition
B.2
.

We now prove part (2). By part (1),
T^{i}(A)
is flat if and only if
T^{i}
is exact. Then part (2) follows from Proposition
B.2
and the following basic fact:
T^{i}
is exact if and only if
T^{i-1}
and
T^{i}
are both right exact.
∎

Corollary B.4
.

Let
\pi:X\to Y
be a proper morphism of schemes over a Noetherian scheme
S
. Suppose that
X
is flat over
S
. Given a point
s\in S
, if
R^{1}(\pi_{s})_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{s}})=0
, then

(1)

\pi_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})|_{Y_{s}}\simeq(\pi_{s})_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{s}})

(2)

\pi_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is flat over a neighborhood of
s\in S
.

Proof.

By part (1) of Theorem
B.3
,
R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is
0
in a neighborhood of
s\in S
. In particular,
R^{1}\pi_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is flat over a neighborhood of
s\in S
. By part (2) of Theorem
B.3
,
\pi_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})|_{Y_{s}}\to(\pi_{s})_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{s}})
is surjective. Then part (1) follows from part (1) of Theorem
B.3
.

For part (2), by part (2) of Theorem
B.3
again,
\pi_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X})
is flat over a neighborhood of
s\in S
(note that condition (
2a
) in Proposition
B.2
holds automatically when
i=0
).
∎

Appendix C
Equivariant line bundles on stacks

In this appendix, we collect some basics of line bundles over
k
-stacks, where
k
is an algebraically closed field. We also prove that for any semi-direct
k
-group
G\rtimes H
, any
G\rtimes H
-equivariant line bundle on a
k
-space
X
can descend to a
H
-equivariant line bundle on the quotient stack
[G\backslash X]
.

Denote by
\mathrm{Aff}_{k}
the category of affine
k
-schemes. A
k
-space (resp.
k
-group) is a functor from
\mathrm{Aff}_{k}^{\mathrm{op}}
to the category of sets (resp. groups) which is a sheaf with respect to the fppf topology, cf.
[
5
,
39
]
.

Let
X
be a
k
-space. Following
[
5
, (3.7)]
, a line bundle
\mathscr{L}
over
X
consists of following data: a line bundle
\mathscr{L}_{\eta}
over
S
for each scheme
S
with a morphism
\eta:S\to X
, a collection of isomorphisms
\{\phi_{\eta,f}:f^{*}\mathscr{L}_{\eta}\simeq\mathscr{L}_{\eta\circ f}\}
for each morphism
f:S^{\prime}\to S
of schemes, subject to obvious compatibility conditions. In fact, this definition coincides with the usual definition of line bundles when
X
is either a scheme or an ind-scheme.

More generally, a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on a stack
Y
is defined to be the following data: a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
over
S
for each scheme
S
and
\eta\in Y(S)
, an isomorphism
\phi_{\theta}:f^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{\eta^{\prime}}
for each morphism
f:S^{\prime}\to S
of schemes and each arrow
\theta:f^{*}\eta\to\eta^{\prime}
in
Y(S^{\prime})
, subject to obvious compatibility conditions, cf.
[
5
, (3.7)]
.

Let
G
be a
k
-group, and let
X
be a
k
-space with a left
G
-action. Denote by
a:G\times X\to X
the action map, and by
\mathrm{pr}:G\times X\to X
the projection map. A line bundle
\mathscr{L}
over
X
is called
G
-equivariant if there is an isomorphism
u:a^{*}\mathscr{L}\simeq\mathrm{pr}^{*}\mathscr{L}
, satisfying the cocycle condition. Let
[G\backslash X]
be the quotient stack. By descent theory, a line bundle over
[G\backslash X]
is the same as a
G
-equivariant line bundle over
X
, cf.
[
5
, Section 7]
.

Suppose there is a
k
-group
H
acting on the
k
-group
G
preserving the group structure, and there is a
(G\rtimes H)
-action on the
k
-space
X
. Then there is an
H
-action on
[G\backslash X]
defined as follows. For any scheme
S
, an
S
-point
\eta
of
[G\backslash X]
is equivalent to a
G
-torsor
\pi:\mathcal{P}\to S
together with a
G
-equivariant morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{P}\to X
. This produces a
G
-equivariant morphism

\tilde{\eta}:\mathcal{P}\to X\times S.

For any
h\in H(S)
, there is a
G
-equivariant map

(C.1)

h:X\times S\to X\times S,

sending
(x,s)
to
(h(s)^{-1}x,s)
. Here the domain
X\times S
is equipped with a new
G
-action by
g\mathbin{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.6}{\textbullet}}}(x,s):=(h(s)gh^{-1}(s)x,s)
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{h}
denote the
G
-torsor
P
with a new
G
-action given by
g\mathbin{\raisebox{0.86108pt}{\scalebox{0.6}{\textbullet}}}p:=h(\pi(p))gh^{-1}(\pi(p))p
. Then, the following composition map is
G
-equivariant

\alpha_{h}:\mathcal{P}_{h}\xrightarrow{\tilde{\eta}}X\times S\xrightarrow{h}X\times S\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{X}}X,

which sends
p
to
h(\pi(p))^{-1}\alpha(p)
. Hence, the
G
-torsor
\mathcal{P}_{h}
over
S
together with the
G
-equivariant morphism
\alpha_{h}:\mathcal{P}_{h}\to X
defines an
S
-point of
[G\backslash X]
, which will be denoted by
h\cdot\eta
. This gives us an
H
-action on
[G\backslash X]
. In this case, a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
over
[G\backslash X]
is called
H
-equivariant if there are isomorphisms
\phi_{h,\eta}:\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{h\cdot\eta}
satisfying the cocycle condition.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
H
be a
k
-group, and let
G
be a
k
-group with an
H
-action preserving the group structure. Suppose
(G\rtimes H)
acts on a
k
-space
X
, and let
\mathscr{L}
be a
(G\rtimes H)
-equivariant line bundle over
X
. Denote by
\mathcal{L}
the line bundle over the quotient stack
[G\backslash X]
descending from
\mathscr{L}
. Then there is an
H
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{L}
.

Proof.

For any
\eta\in[G\backslash X](S)
, we have an morphism
\tilde{\eta}:\mathcal{P}\to X\times S
. Let
\mathscr{L}_{S}
be the pull-back of
\mathscr{L}
via the projection
X\times S\to X
. As a
G
-equivariant line bundle,
\tilde{\eta}^{*}(\mathscr{L}_{S})
exactly descends to the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
over
S
, where
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
is obtained from
\mathcal{L}
via
\eta
.

Since
\mathscr{L}
is
G\rtimes H
-equivariant, its pull-back
\mathscr{L}_{S}
is also
G\rtimes H
-equivariant, i.e. given any scheme
T
, for any
h^{\prime}\in H(T)
,
g\in G(T)
and
x\in(X\times S)(T)
, there are isomorphisms
\phi_{h^{\prime},x}:(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\simeq(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{h^{\prime}x}
and
\psi_{g,x}:(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\simeq(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{gx}
such that the following diagram commutes

(C.2)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 21.44629pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.42781pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.4424pt\raise 6.82222pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{h^{\prime},x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.58025pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.44629pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi_{g,x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.58025pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{h^{\prime}x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.70137pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.81668pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi_{h^{\prime}gh^{\prime-1},h^{\prime}x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.70137pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{gx}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.60124pt\raise-25.17778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{h^{\prime},gx}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.50403pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.50403pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{h^{\prime}gx}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

For any
h\in H(S)
, let
h:X\times S\to X\times S
be the
G
-equivariant morphism as in (
C.1
). Take
h^{\prime}
to be the morphism
h\circ\mathrm{pr}_{S}\circ x:T\to H
. Then
h^{\prime}\cdot x=h\circ x
. Hence

(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}=(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{h\circ x}=(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{h^{\prime}\cdot x}.

Similarly,
(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{X})_{gx}=(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{h^{\prime}\cdot gx}
. Set
\xi_{h,x}=\psi_{h^{\prime}gh^{\prime-1},h^{\prime}x}:(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\simeq(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{X})_{gx}
, and
\zeta_{h,x}=\phi_{h^{\prime},x}:(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\to(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}
.
Then diagram (
C.2
) becomes

(C.3)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 21.44629pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.42781pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.37955pt\raise 6.4472pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\zeta_{h,x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.58026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.44629pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.44444pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi_{g,x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.58026pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.18747pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\xi_{h,x}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.18747pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-17.50403pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{gx}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.53839pt\raise-25.5528pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\zeta_{h,gx}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.50403pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.50403pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{gx}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Note that the data of isomorphisms
\xi_{h,x}:(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\to(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{gx}
of line bundles over
S
defines a
G
-equivariant structure on the line bundle
h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S}
over
X\times S
. Moreover, the data of isomorphism
\zeta_{h,x}:(\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}\to(h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S})_{x}
amounts to an isomorphism

\zeta_{h}:\mathscr{L}_{S}\to h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S}.

Then, the commutativity of the diagram (
C.3
) implies that
\zeta_{h}
is
G
-equivariant.

Pulling back
\zeta_{h}
via
\tilde{\eta}:\mathcal{P}\to X\times S
, we get a
G
-equivariant isomorphism

\tilde{\eta}^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S}\to\tilde{\eta}^{*}h^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S}=(h\circ\tilde{\eta})^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S}.

Note that
\mathrm{pr}_{X}\circ h\circ\tilde{\eta}=\alpha_{h}
. This implies
\widetilde{h\cdot\eta}=h\circ\tilde{\eta}
. Hence, there is an
G
-equivariant isomorphism

\tilde{\eta}^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S}\simeq(\widetilde{h\cdot\eta})^{*}\mathscr{L}_{S},

and it descends to an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{h\cdot\eta}
of line bundles over
S
. One may check this isomorphism satisfies the standard cocycle condition.
∎
